# Assumptions

One line per decision made without the user or the client: `- [date] <question> → <decision> → <reason>`.
Referenced by `docs/TASKS.md`. Superseded entries are struck through, never deleted.

## Phase 1 — planning (2026-09-11)

- [2026-09-11] New billing entities in `Entities.cs` or a new file? → new `SchoolLms.Domain/Billing.cs` → `Entities.cs` is 1077 lines and five agents write billing code in parallel in Phase 1.C; it is the worst merge-conflict surface in the repo.
- [2026-09-11] Convert the whole schema to `uuid`/`date` per SPEC §3? → no, only the new billing tables use real types; billing FKs reference `students(id)` as `text` → Phase 0 shipped the legacy shape on Postgres; converting 53 entities is not Phase 1 work and buys nothing for the money module.
- ~~[2026-09-11] .NET 10 upgrade timing (SPEC §2.1 says Phase 0, it did not happen) → defer to after Phase 1 → a runtime upgrade in the same three weeks as the money module doubles the blast radius of any failure. Confirm at the P1-25 gate.~~ **Superseded — the premise was wrong: the upgrade had already happened.**
- [2026-09-11] .NET 10 upgrade go/no-go (P1-02 acceptance criterion) → **already done, verified 2026-09-11, running in production** → `SchoolLms.Server.csproj` targets `net10.0`, EF Core 10.0.12, Npgsql 10.0.3, Dockerfile builds on `mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/sdk:10.0` and runs on `aspnet:10.0`. Confirmed by `dotnet list package` inside the SDK container. The "defer it" recommendation in TASKS.md P1-02 is therefore moot and needs no decision.
- ~~[2026-09-11] Discount approval threshold (client Q5 unanswered) → 20 % or 500 000 so'm → configuration value, changeable later with an `UPDATE`, not a migration.~~ **Superseded by the client answer (SPEC §8.1 Q5): there is no threshold — every discount needs the director's approval. `billing_settings` therefore carries no threshold column, and P1-08 must not implement one.**
- [2026-09-11] Payment due date and grace period (client Q6 unanswered) → due on the 10th, overdue after the 15th → same: configuration, not schema.
- [2026-09-11] Receipt channel (client Q3 unanswered) → PDF + Telegram only → `TelegramService.SendDocumentAsync` already exists; ESC/POS needs hardware that has not been bought.
- [2026-09-11] Opening cash float (client Q11 unanswered) → 0, cashier may enter one when opening a shift → matches how the desk works today.
- [2026-09-11] Part-month enrolment (client Q12 unanswered) → charge the full month → this is what the current `TuitionService.AccrueMonth` already does; changing it silently would alter existing figures.
- [2026-09-11] Which methods count toward `expected_cash` (client Q13 unanswered) → `cash` only → card and transfer settle to the bank, so counting them would guarantee a false variance every shift.
- [2026-09-11] Advance payment for a whole year (client Q15 unanswered) → hold as unallocated credit, allocate as each month accrues → creating twelve future invoices would make a mid-year price or discount change unfixable without editing invoices.
- [2026-09-11] Opening student debt at go-live (client Q17 unanswered) → start from zero → only demo data exists today; if the client supplies a file it is imported as ordinary invoices through `LedgerService`.
- [2026-09-11] PDF engine → QuestPDF Community, pending licence confirmation in P1-12 → free under 1 M USD annual revenue. If a commercial licence is required, stop and ask the user: that is money spent. **Confirmed in P1-12 (2026-09-11) against the licence text inside the package: the Community License applies, nothing is bought. See the P1-12 section below.**
- [2026-09-11] Q14: one payment covering two siblings? → **No — one receipt per student.**
  Decided by the client. `payments.student_id` stays a single column (SPEC §3.7 unchanged);
  a guardian paying for two children produces two payments. Keeps the schema simple and the
  per-student ledger unambiguous. Revisit only if the cashier reports real friction.

## Phase 1 — P1-02, DB role separation (2026-09-11)

- [2026-09-11] Two roles (SPEC §4.1) or three? → **three**: `schoollms` (bootstrap superuser, kept), `schoollms_owner` (schema owner, migrations), `app_rw` (the app) → SPEC §4.1 says the owner "is the existing `schoollms` role", but that role is the initdb SUPERUSER — a superuser bypasses `REVOKE` even harder than an owner does. `pg_isready`, `pg_dump` and break-glass access still need it, so it stays and is simply never used by the app. TASKS.md P1-02 already names `schoollms_owner`, so the two documents now agree.
- [2026-09-11] How do new tables created by a future migration lose `DELETE` again? → `deploy/init-roles.sql` is re-run after every migration, as a documented mandatory deploy step → `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` grants full CRUD on new tables, so `payments` will arrive in P1-04 with `DELETE` granted. A DB `EVENT TRIGGER` could automate it, but it fires as the table owner and would be invisible machinery that nobody at 3am can debug. A re-run is one line in `deploy/README.md`.
- [2026-09-11] Migration stays automatic on container start (my own DevOps rule says it should be its own step) → keep auto-migrate, but add `Database:AutoMigrate` (default `true`) → flipping it to `false` today would break the existing deploy flow for zero benefit while there is no money in the schema. The flag makes the split a config change, not a code change, when P1-04 lands. Documented in `deploy/README.md`.
- [2026-09-11] Should `app_rw` also lose write access to `__EFMigrationsHistory`? → **no, not in P1-02** → it would be good hardening, but it turns the dev fallback (`Migrator` unset → migrate on `Default`) into a startup failure, and the task explicitly requires that fallback to keep working. Revisit in P1-22 together with the integration test.
- [2026-09-11] Migrator connection uses `Pooling=false` → yes → otherwise Npgsql keeps an idle, fully privileged schema-owner connection open for the entire process lifetime (observed in `pg_stat_activity` during verification) and burns one of the 60 connection slots on the 3 GB server. Migration is a one-shot job; pooling buys nothing.
- [2026-09-11] `deploy/init-roles.sql` reads passwords via psql `\getenv` → requires PostgreSQL 16+ → the stack is pinned to `postgres:17-alpine`, verified working. Keeps secrets out of the SQL file and out of git. If the image is ever downgraded below 16 this script fails loudly at `\getenv`, which is the correct outcome.

## Phase 1 — P1-03, test harness (2026-09-11)

- [2026-09-11] Assertion library: FluentAssertions or plain xUnit `Assert`? → **plain `Assert`** → FluentAssertions 8.x requires a paid licence for commercial projects; buying one is money spent, which needs the user's decision. The free fork (AwesomeAssertions) is another dependency for no functional gain. Zero licence risk beats prettier syntax.
- [2026-09-11] How does the test get a PostgreSQL, given no local .NET SDK and no Docker-in-Docker? → **Testcontainers with the host Docker socket mounted (sibling container) + `TESTCONTAINERS_HOST_OVERRIDE=host.docker.internal`** → a `docker-compose.test.yml` would need `up` and `down` around `dotnet test` and would break the "`dotnet test` runs green from a clean clone" criterion. `TEST_PG_ADMIN_CONNECTION` is still honoured for CI service containers.
- [2026-09-11] `WebApplicationFactory<TEntryPoint>` needs a public entry type, but `Program.cs` uses top-level statements (internal `Program`). → **use `SchoolLms.Server.Controllers.AuthController` as `TEntryPoint`** → the documented fix is to add `public partial class Program { }` to `Program.cs`, but a test must not change production code. WebApplicationFactory only uses `TEntryPoint` to locate the assembly.
- [2026-09-11] One database per test, or one per run? → **one per run, shared** → container + migration + host boot costs ~8 s; repeating it per test would blow the 90 s budget. Isolation comes from every test creating its own uniquely-named data. `PostgresFixture.CreateDatabaseAsync()` gives a pristine database (cloned from a migrated template, ~100 ms) when a test genuinely needs one.
- [2026-09-11] Should the fixture create the `app_rw` role itself, since P1-02 has not merged? → **no — fall back to the owner string and flag it** → a fixture-created role with fixture-written grants would make P1-22 (ledger immutability) pass while testing nothing. `TestDatabase.RequireRealAppRw()` fails such a test loudly instead.
- [2026-09-11] Language of comments in `SchoolLms.Tests` → **Uzbek in code, English in `docs/TESTING.md`** → mirrors the repository as it is: every existing `.cs` file comments in Uzbek, every file under `docs/` is English.

## Phase 1 — P1-04, billing entities + `cashier` role (2026-09-11)

- [2026-09-11] Where do the two configurable payment-date settings (client Q6: `payment_due_day`, `overdue_after_day`) live — new columns on `SchoolMeta`, or their own table? → **own table `billing_settings`, single row, fixed id `…0000b1`** → two reasons, either alone sufficient. (1) `SchoolMeta` lives in `Entities.cs`, and P1-04's acceptance criterion is literally "the diff of `SchoolLms.Domain/Entities.cs` is empty" — five agents write billing code in parallel in Phase 1.C. (2) `SchoolMeta` is already a 40-column grab bag (Telegram token, turnstile password, GPS radius); putting money settings in it would blur the "who may write to a finance table" question that P1-22 has to answer. Adding a setting later is a one-column additive migration.
- [2026-09-11] Billing timestamps: `DateTime` (like every existing entity) or `DateTimeOffset`? → **`DateTimeOffset` → `timestamptz`** → `AppDbContext.OnModelCreating` forces *every* `DateTime` column to `timestamp without time zone`, because the legacy code stores Tashkent wall-clock time. That is wrong for money: `received_at` and shift boundaries without an offset make a Z-report impossible to recompute, and SPEC §7 requires `timestamptz`. The existing loop only matches `DateTime`, so `DateTimeOffset` sidesteps it without touching a single legacy column.
- [2026-09-11] `payments.method` allowed values: SPEC §3.7 SQL says `cash|card|transfer`, client answer Q13 adds Payme/Click/Uzum → **four labels: `cash|card|transfer|online`** → the client answer is newer and explicit. `online` is a *label only* — no provider integration in this or any later phase. Only `cash` counts toward `expected_cash` at shift close.
- [2026-09-11] `discounts` needs a `status` column that SPEC §3.7 does not have → **added `status` (pending|approved|rejected) + `decided_at`** → client answer Q5: every discount needs the director's approval, no threshold. A discount is inert until `approved_by` is set, so accrual filters on `status = 'approved'`. Two DB constraints back it: `approved_by <> created_by` (SPEC §4.5) and `status <> 'approved' or approved_by is not null`.
- [2026-09-11] Check constraints and the `variance` generated column: raw SQL in the migration (as TASKS.md P1-05 lists them) or the EF model? → **EF model (`BillingModel.cs`), raw SQL only for what EF cannot express** → anything in the model lands in the snapshot, so a later `--autogenerate` leaves it alone; anything added by raw SQL is invisible to EF and can be silently dropped by a future migration. Only the allocation trigger and the GRANT/REVOKE stayed raw.
- [2026-09-11] EF configuration for 10 billing entities inside `AppDbContext.OnModelCreating`? → **separate `BillingModel.Apply(b)` in `Infrastructure/Data/BillingModel.cs`, one call site** → same argument that put the entities in `Billing.cs`: `AppDbContext` is a shared file and Phase 1.C is parallel. `AppDbContext` keeps the DbSets (they must be there) and one line of configuration.
- [2026-09-11] `Roles.Cashier` and token revocation → **added `cashier` to the `OnTokenValidated` branch in `Program.cs` (one line)** → without it a cashier fell into the `else { blocked = false; }` arm, so a *deleted* cashier would keep taking payments with an old token. `Program.cs` is P1-15's file, but shipping a money role with broken revocation is not an option; the change is additive and flagged to P1-15's owner.
- [2026-09-11] FK behaviour from billing rows to `users(id)` → **`DeleteBehavior.Restrict` everywhere** → a user who has taken money, approved a discount or posted a ledger entry can no longer be deleted. That is the correct audit behaviour (SPEC §4.6) and it costs nothing today because no billing rows exist yet. `StaffController.Delete` will start returning an FK error for such users once they do — deliberate.
- [2026-09-11] Extra DB guarantees not named in SPEC but cheap and load-bearing → **added**: partial unique index `ux_cash_shifts_one_open_per_cashier` (SPEC §4.2 says a cashier cannot open a second shift — now the database says it, not just the service), and a partial unique index on `payments.reversal_of` (a payment can be reversed at most once; without it the same payment could be "refunded" twice).

## Phase 1 — P1-05, `BillingCore` migration + database guards (2026-09-11)

- [2026-09-11] How do the new money tables lose `UPDATE`/`DELETE` again, given `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` hands every freshly created table to `app_rw` with full CRUD — (a) `REVOKE` inside the migration, or (b) re-run `deploy/init-roles.sql` after every migration (the P1-02 decision)? → **both, with (a) as the primary** → (b) alone is a manual step that produces *no error when forgotten*: the app keeps working and the money protection is simply gone. (a) makes the protection arrive in the same transaction as the `CREATE TABLE`, so it cannot be forgotten. (b) stays because it also covers `access_events` / `point_transactions` (Phase 2 and 4), refreshes passwords, and prints the `owns_in_public = 0` evidence line. The P1-02 entry above is extended, not superseded. Verified: `update payments set amount = 1` as `app_rw` → SQLSTATE 42501, row survives.
- [2026-09-11] The migration's GRANT/REVOKE block when the `app_rw` role does not exist (test harness, pre-P1-02 databases) → **skip with a `RAISE NOTICE`, do not fail** → the DDL has already been applied by that point, so failing would leave a half-migrated database. The notice says plainly that the database has no financial immutability and points at `init-roles.sql`.
- [2026-09-11] Where does the raw SQL live — inline C# string in the migration, or a `.sql` file? → **`.sql` file embedded as an assembly resource (`Migrations/Sql/*.sql`, read by `Migrations/MigrationSql.cs`)** → 120 lines of trigger and grant logic inside a C# string literal cannot be reviewed: no syntax highlighting, escaped quotes, one-line diffs. Financial protection has to be readable. An embedded resource (not a file on disk) is required because migrations run in the published container, where the source tree does not exist; a missing resource throws loudly instead of silently skipping the guards.
- [2026-09-11] `DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS` before `CREATE TRIGGER` (the usual idempotency idiom)? → **no — `CREATE OR REPLACE TRIGGER` (PostgreSQL 14+, stack is 17)** → P1-05's acceptance criterion is that `dotnet ef migrations script` contains zero `DROP`. Keeping that rule absolute is what makes "a `DROP` in `Up()` means stop and read" a usable habit; one tolerated `DROP` and the check stops being scannable. The table is created in the same migration, so the trigger cannot pre-exist anyway.
- [2026-09-11] The allocation trigger in SPEC §3.7 has a concurrency hole (two parallel transactions each see only committed allocations, so both can pass and the payment gets allocated twice). Fix with `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on `payments`? → **no — `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended(payment_id::text, 0))`** → `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` requires the `UPDATE` privilege on `payments`, which `app_rw` deliberately does not have; the lock would therefore fail with 42501 for the very role that needs it. The alternative, a `SECURITY DEFINER` function, adds a privilege-escalation surface for no gain. An advisory lock needs no table privilege and is released at commit.
- [2026-09-11] Seed (five fee categories + the settings row): EF `HasData`, application startup, or SQL in the migration? → **SQL in the migration, with hand-written stable UUIDs, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`** → `HasData` would make the rows part of the model, so a later edit to a category name becomes a migration. Startup seeding would need `app_rw` to insert reference data. Stable ids (`…c1`…`…c5`, `…b1`) matter because a category id read from a production log has to be findable in a local database.
- [2026-09-11] How is "`app_rw` cannot UPDATE/DELETE `payments`" proven, given the test harness deliberately does not create `app_rw` (docs/TESTING.md §4)? → **`tools/verify-billing-guards.sh`** → it starts a throwaway Postgres 17, applies the real `deploy/init-roles.sql` at initdb time, runs the migration as `schoollms_owner`, and then asserts 42501 plus the trigger, the dual-control constraint, the single-open-shift index, seed idempotency and "zero `DROP` in the migration script". This reproduces the production path exactly, which the in-process harness by design cannot. It does not replace P1-22 — see the handoff note in `docs/TESTING.md`.

## Phase 1 — P1-06, frozen contracts (2026-09-11)

- [2026-09-11] TASKS.md says the billing DTOs go into `SchoolLms.Application/Dtos/Dtos.cs`, but that file already declares `PaymentDto` (line 591, salary/ledger history) and `PaymentRequest` (line 37, the legacy student payment) — and billing needs both names. → **new file `Dtos/BillingDtos.cs` with its own namespace `SchoolLms.Application.Dtos.Billing`** → renaming the billing types to `BillingPaymentDto` / `BillingPaymentRequest` would uglify the whole module to work around a name clash that C# namespaces exist to solve. A controller that needs both aliases one. It also keeps `Dtos.cs` (993 lines) out of the Phase 1.C merge path, for the same reason `Billing.cs` was split from `Entities.cs`.
- [2026-09-11] Billing request DTOs are named `…Request`, not `…Payload` as the rest of `Dtos.cs` does → **uniform `Request` suffix inside the billing namespace** → SPEC §4.4 (`cashier_id` / `created_by` / `approved_by` never in a request body) has to be *mechanically* checkable, and one suffix makes it a single command. Filter types are `…Query` and are deliberately exempt: `PaymentQuery.CashierId` is "show me this cashier's payments", not "I am this cashier" — a filter never reaches a written row. Verified at P1-06: 13 `Request` types, 0 identity fields.
- [2026-09-11] Should response DTOs carry raw `created_by` / `approved_by` ids, or resolved names? → **names (`CreatedByName`, `ApprovedByName`, `CashierName`), plus the raw id only where the UI genuinely needs it (`CashShiftDto.CashierId`, `PaymentDto.CashierId`)** → every screen that shows a discount or a shift shows a person's name; returning the id alone would make each of the four parallel frontend tasks fetch and join the user list itself, which is four N+1 loops waiting to happen.
- [2026-09-11] Where are `payable` / `paid` / `remaining` / `debt` computed — client or server? → **server; the DTOs carry them precomputed** → money in JavaScript is float64. A `reduce` over invoice amounts in the browser produces tiyin drift that disagrees with the ledger, and the disagreement surfaces as a support call, not as a test failure. The rule is written at the top of both `BillingDtos.cs` and the TS block: the UI displays, it does not compute.
- [2026-09-11] `Roles.FinanceStaff` was committed in P1-04 as `cashier,admin,superadmin,staff`; corrected here to **`admin,superadmin`** → SPEC §4.3 has no `staff` column at all, so including it invented access the spec does not grant; and the cashier is excluded from every back-office row of that table. The legacy `staff` + `finance` permission path is untouched — it keeps working through `AdminPermAttribute` on the old `FinanceController` until P1-21 retires it.
- [2026-09-11] `FinanceAction.EditOrDeletePayment` exists in the matrix with an **empty** role list → SPEC §4.3 states "Edit or delete a payment — impossible for anyone". Modelling that as a row (rather than as the absence of a row) means the rule is written down where P1-22 can assert it, and that `RolesFor` returns empty — deny — for any action whose rule someone forgets to add. Fail-closed by construction.
- [2026-09-11] Client API stubs: throw, or return empty/mock data? → **throw `NotImplementedError` carrying the endpoint and the owning task id** → an empty array makes a half-built screen look finished, which is the expensive failure mode; a thrown error shows on screen and names the task (`P1-11`) that still owes the endpoint. The real one-line axios call is written in a comment directly above each stub, so the 1.C owner swaps the body without touching the signature.
- [2026-09-11] Adding `cashier` to the TS `Role` union broke three `Record<Role, …>` maps in `config/navigation.ts` (P1-20's file) and `npm run build` is a P1-06 acceptance criterion → **added the three minimal entries** (a one-item nav, `/cashier` home, the "Kassir" label) → the alternative, widening the maps to `Partial<Record<…>>`, would silently drop nav entries for every role. P1-20 replaces the placeholder with the real cashier workspace; flagged in the handoff.

## Phase 1 — P1-07, `LedgerService` (2026-09-11)

- [2026-09-11] `ReverseAsync` takes ONE `entryId`, but reversing a single line of a balanced set would leave the journal unbalanced. How is the set identified, given the schema has no batch column and is now frozen? → **a posting batch is `(ref_type, ref_id)`; `PostAsync` refuses a call whose lines disagree on either, and stamps a synthetic `ref_id` when the caller has none (manual adjustment)** → this makes every batch identifiable without a schema change, so `ReverseAsync` can mirror the whole set. The index `ix_ledger_entries_ref_type_ref_id` already supports the lookup. The constraint "one call = one business event" is a real modelling rule, not a workaround: a caller needing two events calls twice.
- [2026-09-11] Reversal entries: keep the original `ref_type`, or use `reversal`? → **`ref_type = 'reversal'`, `ref_id` = the original's** → the reversal lines then fall outside the original batch's own `(ref_type, ref_id)` query, so "find the batch" and "has it been reversed" stay two simple, non-overlapping questions. `reversal_of` still points at the individual original line.
- [2026-09-11] The reversal's `entry_date`: the original's date, or today? → **today** → back-dating a correction into a period that has already been reported silently rewrites a number somebody has already read. The correction belongs to the day it was made.
- [2026-09-11] Rounding: reject amounts with more than 2 decimals, or round them? → **round to 2 decimals first, then check the balance on the rounded values** → the column is `numeric(14,2)`, so validating the unrounded values would let a set that balances in memory be stored unbalanced. Rounding first means what is validated is exactly what is persisted; if rounding breaks the balance the caller gets a loud error rather than a silent half-tiyin drift.
- [2026-09-11] Dual control on `ReverseAsync` → **the approver may not be any of the batch's authors** → SPEC §4.5 lists payment reversal and manual ledger adjustment as always requiring a second person. Checking every line's `created_by` (not just the anchor's) closes the case where a batch was somehow written by two people.
- [2026-09-11] `AppClock` needed an offset-aware accessor for the new `timestamptz` columns; the first attempt returned UTC+5 and Npgsql rejected it (`"only offset 0 (UTC) is supported"`). → **`AppClock.NowInstant` returns `DateTimeOffset.UtcNow`, plus `ToLocal` / `LocalDateOf` for display and day-grouping** → `timestamptz` stores an instant, not an offset, so UTC is the correct and only accepted representation. Putting it on `AppClock` (one additive property) rather than leaving each Phase 1.C service to convert for itself is the point: five agents writing five conversions would produce five subtly different ones.
- [2026-09-11] `LedgerService.PostAsync` calls `SaveChangesAsync` itself, so a payment write (P1-11) would be two commits. → **added `BeginTransactionAsync` to `IAppDbContext`** → a crash between the two commits would leave a payment with no ledger entry, which is exactly the kind of discrepancy nobody can reconstruct afterwards. The alternative (making the caller save) would have let a standalone `PostAsync` silently do nothing. Recorded for Phase 1.C in `docs/PENDING_WIRING.md`.
- [2026-09-11] `Accounts.SettlementFor(method)` (cash → `cash`, everything else → `bank`) is defined by delegating to `PaymentMethod.CountsAsCash` rather than by its own `switch` → "is this cash?" must have exactly one answer in the codebase; the shift's `expected_cash` and the ledger posting have to agree or every shift shows a phantom variance.
- [2026-09-11] `LedgerService` is not registered in DI → **deliberate; recorded in `docs/PENDING_WIRING.md` for P1-15** → `Program.cs` is P1-15's file and Phase 1.B should not start claiming it. Nothing breaks at build time; the first controller that injects `ILedgerService` fails at request time until the one line is added.

## Phase 1 — P1-08, fee catalog, subscriptions, discounts (2026-09-11)

- [2026-09-11] `docs/TASKS.md` P1-08 still says "a discount above the configured threshold returns `409 approval_required`", and names a default threshold of 20 % / 500 000 so'm. → **No threshold exists. Every discount is created `pending` and the create response is `200` with `status = "pending"`, never 409** → client answer SPEC §8.1 Q5 supersedes the task text: *"Every discount needs the director's approval — no threshold."* With a threshold, a below-threshold discount took effect immediately; without one, a discount is inert from birth, so waiting for approval is the normal flow, not an exception — and 409 is the wrong code for a normal flow. Accrual (P1-09) filters on `status = 'approved'`; `ck_discounts_approved_has_approver` and `ck_discounts_approver_differs` back it in the database.
- [2026-09-11] Approval is restricted to `superadmin` by `[FinanceRole(FinanceAction.ApproveDiscount)]`. Should `DiscountService` check the role again? → **yes — it loads the approver and rejects a non-`superadmin` with `approver_not_director`** → the controller attribute is one line that a later refactor or a new endpoint can omit without any test noticing. A money rule must not depend on one layer remembering. Same argument for the self-approval check, which exists in three places: the service, the DB check constraint, and the §4.3 matrix.
- [2026-09-11] `RejectDiscountRequest.Reason` has nowhere to go: `discounts` has no `decision_note` column (schema frozen in P1-05) and `DiscountDto` has no field for it. Overwrite `discounts.reason`? → **no — the rejection reason goes to `audit_log` only** → `discounts.reason` is the *requester's* justification; overwriting it destroys why the discount was asked for, which is the one thing the director needs when the same request comes back next month. The frozen DTO cannot show a rejection reason anyway, so audit is the only honest home for it.
- [2026-09-11] On reject, is `approved_by` set to the rejecting director? → **yes; the column means "who decided"** → otherwise a rejected row records no decision-maker at all, and the DB constraint `approved_by <> created_by` is enforced for rejection too. Consequence, accepted deliberately: a director cannot reject a discount they requested themselves; they leave it pending instead.
- [2026-09-11] "The first tuition subscription is pre-filled from `SchoolClass.MonthlyFee`" — where does the pre-fill happen, given `CreateSubscriptionRequest.MonthlyAmount` is a non-nullable `decimal` so "omitted" and "zero" are indistinguishable? → **both: `GET /api/admin/billing/subscriptions/default` returns the suggestion for the form, and `CreateAsync` falls back to the class fee when the amount is 0, the category is `tuition`, and the student has no tuition subscription yet** → the endpoint is the primary path (a pre-fill is a UI act). The server-side fallback exists because a zero-price tuition subscription would otherwise be a way to make a student free *without the director's approval*, which is precisely the hole Q5 closes — free tuition is a 100 % discount, and that needs a signature. The substitution is never silent: the response DTO returns the stored amount and the audit line names the source ("summa manbai: 8-A sinfining oylik to'lovi"). Only the **first** subscription in the category gets a suggestion; after that the price lives on the subscription and a class-fee change no longer moves it — raising a class fee must not silently re-price 300 existing contracts.
- [2026-09-11] Overlapping subscriptions: where is "one price per (student, category, month)" enforced? → **in `SubscriptionService` only, for now** → the database equivalent is an `EXCLUDE USING gist` constraint that needs the `btree_gist` extension and therefore a migration, and P1-08 owns no migration file. Recorded in `docs/PENDING_WIRING.md` §10 for the next migration owner. This is a genuine gap under concurrency, not a design choice.
- [2026-09-11] `ISubscriptionService` has no frozen counterpart in `IBillingServices.cs` (P1-06 froze five interfaces; subscriptions was not one). → **the interface is declared in `SchoolLms.Application/Billing/SubscriptionService.cs`** → adding a type to the frozen file would have collided with the four other Phase 1.C agents editing in parallel, for the same reason `Billing.cs` was split from `Entities.cs`. Every other P1-06 convention is kept: DTOs from `Dtos.Billing`, `actorId` as a separate parameter, no generic CRUD.
- [2026-09-11] How does a service-layer rule violation become an HTTP status, given the project has no exception-to-status convention? → **a dedicated `BillingRuleException` (fault kind + machine code) plus a `[BillingFault]` exception filter** → the obvious alternative, catching `InvalidOperationException` in the controller, would also catch "Unable to resolve service for type `IDiscountService`" — the exact state of the app until P1-15 wires DI — and report a broken deployment as a 409 business response. The fault kind is an enum, not an HTTP number, so the Application layer stays HTTP-free (SPEC §2.2). Error body is `{code, message}` and not `ProblemDetails`, because the whole frontend reads `err.response.data.message`.
- [2026-09-11] Filtering discounts by `categoryId`: return only rows with that `category_id`, or also the global ones (`category_id is null`, "all categories" per SPEC §3.7)? → **also the global ones** → the filter answers "which discounts *apply to* this category", which is what accrual (P1-09) needs. The narrow reading would make a global discount invisible to the per-category accrual and silently overcharge the student.
- [2026-09-11] A cashier reading the catalog — allowed? → **no: the whole controller is `[Authorize(Roles = Roles.FinanceStaff)]` (admin + superadmin), reads included** → SPEC §4.3 has no row granting the cashier anything here, not even read. The acceptance criterion only demanded 403 on writes; denying reads as well follows the table rather than inventing an allowance it does not contain.
- [2026-09-11] EF could not translate `Where`/`OrderBy` applied after a constructor projection (`select new DiscountDto(...)`) — it throws at query time, not at build time, and the first test run hit it on 20 tests. → **filter and sort on the entity columns, project into an anonymous type inside the same query, build the DTO in memory after `ToListAsync`** → still one SQL statement with the sort done by the database, and no N+1; the DTO constructor simply stops being part of the expression tree. Noted here because the failure mode (compiles fine, dies at runtime) will bite P1-09…P1-12 writing the same kind of projection.
- [2026-09-11] `BillingCatalogTests` needs the two services in DI to test the happy path over HTTP, but `Program.cs` belongs to P1-15. → **a derived factory in the test file (`fixture.Api.WithWebHostBuilder(… AddScoped …)`)** → touching `Fixtures/ApiFactory.cs` would have put P1-08 into a file the other parallel tasks also use. The RBAC tests need no such thing: authorization filters run before the controller is constructed, so 401/403 are already reachable today.

## Phase 1 — P1-09, invoices + per-category accrual (2026-09-11)

- [2026-09-11] A student has two approved discounts that both apply to one invoice (one for the category, one for "all categories") — stack them, or pick one? → **pick one: the most specific wins (category-specific over global), ties broken by the later `starts_on`, then `created_at`** → the legacy model had exactly one discount per student (`Student.DiscountPct` + `.DiscountAmount`, applied together), so "one winning row" reproduces today's figures, which P1-23 has to match. Silent stacking would also let two innocent-looking 60 % approvals add up to a free year, and the director who approved the second one would never see it.
- [2026-09-11] An invoice whose payable is 0 (100 % discount, or a free subscription) → **the row is still written, its status derives to `paid`, and it gets NO ledger pair** → the journal rejects a zero-amount line (`ck_ledger_entries_amount: amount > 0`) and rightly so: there is no receivable and no revenue to record. Dropping the invoice row instead would hide the student from the monthly report, which is the opposite of what a 100 % discount should do — the legacy `AccrueMonth` kept the row for the same reason.
- [2026-09-11] `invoices.status` at creation: set `'open'` literally, or derive it? → **always derived, `InvoiceService.StatusFor(payable, allocated)`, including at creation (allocated = 0)** → the acceptance criterion is "status moves `open → partial → paid` from allocation totals only; nothing sets it directly". One function means P1-11 cannot invent a second, subtly different rule when it records a payment; it also makes the zero-payable case fall out of the arithmetic instead of needing a special case.
- [2026-09-11] `entry_date` of the accrual's ledger pair: today, or the month being accrued? → **the first day of the accrued month** → a July month back-filled in September belongs to July's P&L. Booking it in September would make the monthly income report depend on when the job happened to run, and nobody could reconcile the two afterwards. (The *reversal* of such a pair still books today — that rule was set in P1-07 and is unchanged.)
- [2026-09-11] The accrual job is unattended, but `ledger_entries.created_by` is `not null` and references `users(id)` (SPEC §4.4). Who is the author? → **the first `superadmin` by id, falling back to `admin`; with neither, the tick is skipped with a warning** → a dedicated `system` user would read better in the journal but needs a migration, and the migration chain is a shared file that P1-09 may not touch. Recorded for P1-15 in `docs/PENDING_WIRING.md`. A database with no admin also has no subscriptions, so the skipped tick loses nothing.
- [2026-09-11] Two subscriptions for the same `(student, category)` overlap one month (the old one ended on the 10th, the new one started on the 11th) — the unique index allows only one invoice. → **the one with the later `starts_on` wins, i.e. the current price** → charging the superseded price for the month the price changed is the version that generates a complaint. P1-08 already rejects overlapping *active* subscriptions, so this only arises for a genuine mid-month switch.
- [2026-09-11] Does a payment that was later reversed still count toward an invoice's `paid`? → **no — `PaidByInvoiceAsync` excludes both the reversal row and the payment it reverses** → `payment_allocations.amount > 0` is a check constraint and the table is insert-only, so a storno can neither write a negative allocation nor delete the original one. The only correct place to undo it is the query that sums allocations; doing it anywhere else would leave a reversed payment looking like it still cleared the debt.
- [2026-09-11] "Overdue" boundary when the admin changes `overdue_after_day` after invoices already exist → **`max(invoice.due_on, overdue_after_day of that period month)`** → reading the setting live is what the client asked for (Q6), but lowering it must not make an invoice overdue *before* the due date printed on it. The floor keeps the two settings from contradicting each other on historical rows.
- [2026-09-11] `InvoiceQuery` (frozen in P1-06) has no paging, and an unfiltered list is ~36 000 rows for a full school year. → **`ListAsync` orders newest-month-first and caps at `MaxListRows = 2000`** → returning everything is how a finance page becomes a 30-second request. A cap silently truncates, which is why it is written in the XML doc, in `docs/PENDING_WIRING.md` and here: the real fix is two optional fields on `InvoiceQuery`, which the frozen-contract rules explicitly allow as additive.
- [2026-09-11] Where does the discount formula live, given P1-08 (`DiscountService`) and P1-09 (accrual) were written in parallel and P1-09 may create only two files? → **`DiscountMath` (static) at the bottom of `InvoiceService.cs`; P1-08 delegates to it** → injecting `IDiscountService` into the accrual would have made P1-09 unbuildable and untestable until P1-08 merged. A static helper keeps exactly one copy of the arithmetic, which is the only property that actually matters here.
- [2026-09-11] `VoidAsync` order of operations: mark the row `void` first, or reverse the ledger first? → **ledger first** → `LedgerService.ReverseAsync` enforces dual control (§4.5) and throws when the person voiding is the one who ran the accrual. With the status set first, the database rolls back correctly but EF leaves the *tracked* entity as `void` in memory, so the next (legitimate) attempt on the same context fails with "already voided" while the row is alive in the database. Caught by `Bekor_qilingan_oy_…` before it reached anyone.
- [2026-09-11] Which months does `AccrueDueAsync` fill? → **`AcademicYearStartMonthAsync` … current month, extended backwards if a subscription starts earlier** → same shape as the legacy `AccrueDue` (which extended backwards for early enrolments), and it reuses `TuitionService.MonthRange` / `NextMonth` unchanged as TASKS.md §1.3 requires. Months where no subscription is active produce zero rows, so the extra iterations are cheap.
- [2026-09-11] Test isolation for accrual tests, given the suite shares one database → **each test creates its own database from the migrated template (~100 ms)** → accrual counts every subscription in the database, so "how many invoices were written" would otherwise depend on other tests' students; and the due-date test *updates* the single `billing_settings` row, which would quietly change the figures P1-10 and P1-11 assert later.

## Phase 1 — P1-11, payment intake / split / reversal (2026-09-11)

- [2026-09-11] `docs/TASKS.md` P1-11 names `POST /api/cash/payments` and `POST /api/admin/payments/{id}/reverse`, but the **frozen** P1-06 client contract (`schoollms.client/src/api/services/payments.ts`) calls `/cashier/payments` and `/admin/billing/payments/{id}/reverse`. → **serve both paths from the same action** (two route attributes, one method body) → both documents are binding: TASKS.md is the acceptance criterion the task is graded on, and the TS stubs are frozen for four parallel frontend tasks. Picking one would have silently broken the other. Cost is one attribute line per action; removing either path later is a breaking change. Recorded for P1-16 in `docs/PENDING_WIRING.md`.
- [2026-09-11] Which shift does a **storno** row belong to — the original payment's, or the approver's? → **the approver's own open shift; no open shift → 409 `no_open_shift`** → the cash physically leaves today's drawer, and `payments.cash_shift_id` is `not null`. Writing into the original (possibly closed) shift would retroactively change a Z-report that has already been signed off, and `cash_shifts.variance` is a stored generated column that nobody can correct afterwards. The price is that an admin must open a shift to hand money back — which is exactly what happens at the desk.
- [2026-09-11] A reversal cannot delete `payment_allocations` (immutable table), so how does an invoice stop counting as paid? → **the storno row gets no allocations of its own; "paid" is defined once, in `PaymentService.EffectiveAllocations()`, as allocations whose payment is neither a storno nor reversed** → the alternative (mirrored negative-signed allocations) needs a sign convention that every future query must remember, and `payment_allocations.amount` has a `> 0` check constraint. `invoices.status` is recomputed from that sum on both the accept and the reverse path, so downstream code can read the status column directly.
- [2026-09-11] Allocating more than an invoice's remaining balance → **rejected, 400 `allocation_exceeds_invoice`** → the client answer on advance payments (Q15) says the surplus is held as *unallocated credit*, not stuffed into an invoice. Allowing an overpaid invoice would make `status = 'paid'` ambiguous and let a cashier hide a surplus inside a line.
- [2026-09-11] Ledger rows per payment: two, or two plus one per category? → **exactly two: `debit cash|bank / credit receivable`** → the acceptance criterion says two, and revenue per category is recognised when the invoice is accrued (P1-09, `debit receivable / credit revenue:<category>`). Recognising it again at payment time would double-count income. A five-way split therefore still produces two journal rows and five allocation rows.
- [2026-09-11] SPEC §4.4 says identity fields in the request body are *rejected*, but `System.Text.Json` silently drops unknown members. → **the two write endpoints bind `JsonElement`, scan the top-level property names against a deny list, return 400 `identity_in_body`, then deserialise** → `[JsonExtensionData]` would have meant duplicating the frozen DTOs in the controller, and buffering the request stream needs middleware in `Program.cs`, which P1-11 must not touch. Only the top level is scanned; nested objects are allocation lines, which carry no identity.
- [2026-09-11] Who may **read** payments? SPEC §4.3 has no "view payments" row. → **gate on `FinanceAction.AcceptPayment` (cash desk), and narrow a cashier to their own rows server-side (another cashier's receipt returns 404, not 403)** → a cashier must be able to reload the receipt just issued, but §4.3 denies them any cross-cashier view; 404 rather than 403 because the existence of a receipt is itself information.
- [2026-09-11] Database-level refusals (allocation trigger, unique index on `reversal_of`) surface as `DbUpdateException`, but `Npgsql` is not referenced by `SchoolLms.Application` and adding it would touch a shared `.csproj` that P1-12 is editing. → **match the stable markers in the exception message chain: the trigger's own text (fixed in `billing_guards.sql`) and the index name** → index names appear in the message in every locale, and the trigger text is documented as the contract P1-11 relies on. The service translates both into the same `PaymentException` codes the application path produces, so the caller sees one behaviour whichever layer caught it.
- [2026-09-11] `ListAsync` has no paging in the frozen `PaymentQuery`. → **hard cap of 1000 rows, newest first, documented on the method and in the controller XML comment** → the real callers (one shift, one student, one day) are far below it; an uncapped query would be fine at 50 rows and fatal at 50 000 on a 3 GB server. Aggregate reporting is P1-13's job, not this endpoint's.
- [2026-09-11] Money amounts with more than two decimals → **rejected (400), not rounded** → the column is `numeric(14,2)`. Rounding silently would store a different number from the one the cashier confirmed on screen, and nobody would ever find the difference.

## Phase 1 — P1-10, cash shifts + gapless receipt numbers (2026-09-11)

- [2026-09-11] Gapless `NextReceiptNoAsync` under 50 concurrent callers: per-shift `pg_advisory_xact_lock`, or insert-retry on the unique violation? → **advisory lock** → three independent reasons. (1) The method *hands out* the number; the `payments` insert happens in the caller (P1-11), so the 23505 arrives in different code — retrying would mean wrapping every payment in a `SAVEPOINT` or replaying the whole payment transaction, splitting one rule across two files while the signature hides it. (2) Retry does not scale here: without a lock all 50 transactions read the same `max + 1` under READ COMMITTED, so 49 fail, then 48, … — O(n²) with no bounded worst case at a live cash desk. (3) The repository already made this exact choice for the allocation trigger (`Migrations/Sql/billing_guards.sql`) for a reason that applies verbatim: `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` needs `UPDATE` on `payments`, which `app_rw` deliberately does not have (42501), while an advisory lock needs no table privilege and is released at commit. Proven by mutation: with `LockShiftAsync` stubbed to a no-op, the 50-caller test fails on a duplicate-key violation.
- [2026-09-11] The advisory-lock key is `hashtextextended('cash_shift_receipt:<uuid>', 0)`, not the bare shift id → the 64-bit advisory space is shared database-wide, and `billing_guards.sql` already hashes `payment_id` into it. A collision would not corrupt anything, but it would make two unrelated operations wait on each other with no way to explain why.
- [2026-09-11] `NextReceiptNoAsync` outside a transaction: silently work, or throw? → **throw** → `pg_advisory_xact_lock` is released at commit, so without a transaction it is released immediately and the receipt number stops being gapless *while the code still looks correct*. A silent loss of a financial guarantee is the worst failure mode available here.
- [2026-09-11] Which payments belong to a shift for `expected_cash` and the Z-report — `payments.cash_shift_id`, or a time window on `opened_at … closed_at`? → **`cash_shift_id` only, on both sides** → `ledger_entries` has no shift column, so a time window would sweep in a *second* cashier's cash movements whenever two shifts overlap. Keying both sides off the same payment set makes `opening_float + (Z-report cash row) == expected_cash` an invariant rather than a coincidence; it is asserted in `CashShiftServiceTests`.
- [2026-09-11] `CloseShiftRequest.Note` has nowhere to go — `cash_shifts` has no note column and adding one is a migration P1-10 may not write. → **write it to `audit_log`** (`entity_type = 'CashShift'`, `action = 'close'`, with a `before`/`after` snapshot) → SPEC §4.6 already requires every financial write to land in `audit_log`, and "50 ming kam chiqdi, ertaga qaytaraman" is needed exactly when the variance is being investigated. Dropping it silently was the only alternative.
- [2026-09-11] `CloseShiftRequest.CountedCash` is a non-nullable `decimal` in the frozen DTO, so a missing field binds to `0` and MVC validation accepts it — but `0` is a legitimate count (no cash payments, no float). TASKS.md requires 400 without it. → **the close endpoint binds `JsonElement` and checks the property is present, then constructs the frozen `CloseShiftRequest`** → the alternative (making the field nullable) changes a contract five parallel agents depend on. The parse helper is ~20 lines and sits next to the reason it exists.
- [2026-09-11] "Closing another cashier's shift → 403": enforce in the controller (which knows the roles) or in the service (whose frozen signature has no role parameter)? → **in the service, by reading `users.role` for `closedByUserId`** → the `FinanceRole` gate answers "may this role touch the cash desk", not "is this shift yours"; putting ownership in the controller would leave the service usable by any future caller. One extra indexed lookup, which also supplies the actor name for the audit row.
- [2026-09-11] `GET /api/cash/shifts/current` with no open shift → **204 No Content**, though the P1-06 client stub comment promises a JSON `null` → ASP.NET turns `Ok(null)` into 204 anyway, and the codebase already uses `NoContent()`; forcing a literal `null` body needs a `JsonResult` hack. The frontend change is one line and is written out in `docs/PENDING_WIRING.md` §11.
- [2026-09-11] Route prefix: TASKS.md P1-10 says `/api/cash/shifts/...`, the P1-06 client stub guessed `/cashier/shifts/...` and `/admin/billing/shifts`. → **follow TASKS.md** → its acceptance criteria name the paths literally, and the stubs are commented-out one-liners that P1-16/P1-20 rewrite anyway. Mismatch recorded in `docs/PENDING_WIRING.md` §11.
- [2026-09-11] `Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Relational` added to `SchoolLms.Application.csproj` — a shared file, which Phase 1.C tasks were told not to touch → **unavoidable and additive** → the Application project referenced only the EF core package, so `Database.ExecuteSqlRawAsync` (the advisory lock) did not compile. The assembly was already loaded at runtime via the Npgsql provider; this only makes it visible to the compiler, and adds no provider dependency. Flagged for the orchestrator in `docs/PENDING_WIRING.md` §10.
- [2026-09-11] `ListAsync` caps at 500 rows (newest first) → each returned shift also costs a grouped read over `payments`; an unbounded query would be fine at 50 shifts and pathological at 50 000. Shifts run 1–2 per day, so 500 is over a year of history, and the director's dashboard filters by date anyway.
- [2026-09-11] Date filtering in `CashShiftQuery` is two-stage (a ±1 day window in SQL, then an exact Tashkent-day filter in memory) → `opened_at` is `timestamptz` and "which Tashkent calendar day" is not expressible in the LINQ provider without a raw cast. The rows are already being materialised to build DTOs, so the exact pass is free — and the bug it prevents (UTC+5 rolls the day at 19:00) only shows up in evening shifts.

## Phase 1 — P1-12, receipt PDF + Telegram delivery (2026-09-11)

- [2026-09-11] **PDF engine — the money question.** QuestPDF Community was *recommended* by TASKS.md but had to be verified, because a commercial licence would mean money spent. → **QuestPDF 2026.8.0 under the Community License — free, nothing to buy** → verified against the licence text shipped *inside the package* (`LICENSE.md`, "License Selection Guide" v3.0, effective 6 July 2026), not against a blog post. Eligibility category (6) "Small businesses": *an organisation with annual gross revenue under USD 1,000,000 in its most recently completed fiscal year* — a single private school is far below that. The two disqualifiers do not apply either: the school is neither a public-sector entity nor a publicly traded company. Obligations under the Community License are things we already do: do not remove QuestPDF's proprietary notices, do not resell the library itself, do not build a competing PDF SDK. If the school's revenue ever crosses USD 1M there is a 90-day transition window — that is a business decision for the owner, not a code change, and it is the only thing that could turn this into a paid dependency.
- [2026-09-11] Alternatives considered before settling on QuestPDF → **PdfSharpCore (MIT) rejected; it is the "free with no conditions at all" option and was the one to beat** → PdfSharpCore has no embedded font: on `mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/aspnet:10.0` there are no system fonts at all, so it needs either a custom `IFontResolver` plus a `.ttf` committed to the repository, or `apt-get install fonts-*` in the Dockerfile — and the Dockerfile belongs to another task. QuestPDF embeds Lato and ships its own Skia build, which was verified end to end: the receipt renders on both `sdk:10.0` and the production `aspnet:10.0` image with **zero** extra apt packages. A licence that we plainly qualify for beat a licence-free library that needs deployment surgery.
- [2026-09-11] Where is `QuestPDF.Settings.License = LicenseType.Community` set, given the library refuses to render without it and `Program.cs` belongs to P1-15? → **in the static constructor of `ReceiptDocument`** → it runs before the first document is ever created, cannot be forgotten, and sits in the same file as the only code that uses the library. A startup line in `Program.cs` would be one more thing to remember during wiring, and it would be silently missing in unit tests.
- [2026-09-11] The published output grows by ~83 MB: NuGet copies QuestPDF's native Skia for **all eight** RIDs (win/linux/osx × arch) and the container uses one of them → **accepted as is; no RID trimming** → the obvious fix (`dotnet publish -r linux-x64`) is a Dockerfile change, and the clever fix (an MSBuild target that strips `runtimes/*`) would break local development on macOS arm64 the first time someone ran the receipt code outside Docker. Flagged as an optional image-size optimisation in `docs/PENDING_WIRING.md`.
- [2026-09-11] `QuestPDF.Settings.CheckIfAllTextGlyphsAreAvailable` — turn it on so missing glyphs are caught? → **no, leave it at the default (`false`)** → with it on, a single character the font lacks (a Cyrillic name, an odd apostrophe pasted from Word) throws and the receipt cannot be produced *at all*. The money has already been taken at that point. A receipt with one placeholder box beats no receipt; Lato covers Latin, Latin Extended and Cyrillic, so the case is rare anyway.
- [2026-09-11] How does a test assert "the receipt number is in the PDF", given PDF text lives in a Flate-compressed content stream encoded as subset-font glyph ids (a plain byte search finds nothing, and a real text extractor means parsing `ToUnicode` CMaps)? → **the number also goes into the PDF metadata `Title`, which Skia writes as a literal ASCII string (`/Title (Chek 1042)`), and the test searches for that** → verified empirically before writing the test. This is not a test-only crutch: the metadata title is what a browser shows in the tab and what a file manager shows in properties, so a receipt that does not carry its number there is genuinely worse. The stamp text `BEKOR QILINGAN` is checked the same way (it is pure ASCII on purpose) plus "the cancelled PDF differs from and is larger than the normal one".
- [2026-09-11] Does `GET /api/receipts/{id}.pdf` also push the receipt to Telegram? → **no — rendering is a pure read; sending is a separate `POST /api/receipts/{id}/telegram`** → a GET with a side effect means the parent gets a new message every time an admin opens the receipt, every time the browser prefetches, every time someone refreshes. The automatic send belongs at the one moment the payment is accepted, i.e. in P1-11's `AcceptAsync` after the transaction commits (exact snippet in `docs/PENDING_WIRING.md`).
- [2026-09-11] `SendToGuardianAsync` when the payment id does not exist, or the database throws → **the method never throws, ever; every path logs and returns `false`** → P1-11 calls it from the payment flow, and an exception crossing that boundary could roll back a committed payment. "Money accepted" and "receipt delivered" are two different facts, and the expensive failure is letting the second one undo the first. Consequence: the `POST` endpoint cannot answer 404 for an unknown payment (it returns `delivered = false`); `RenderPdfAsync` *does* throw `KeyNotFoundException`, so the `GET` gives a proper 404.
- [2026-09-11] Is the payment reversed — read it from `PaymentDto.ReversedBy` (P1-11 fills it) or from the database? → **from the database, one extra query** → a missing `BEKOR QILINGAN` stamp makes a cancelled payment look valid. That failure is silent, it points the wrong way (it favours whoever cancelled the payment), and it would depend on another agent remembering to populate one nullable field. The query is `where id = @id or reversal_of = @id` — one round trip, both rows, both indexed.
- [2026-09-11] A payment whose allocations do not cover the full amount (advance payment, SPEC §8.1 Q15) → **the unallocated remainder is printed as its own line ("Avans (keyingi oylarga)"), so the printed lines always sum to the printed total** → otherwise the parent reads a receipt whose rows do not add up to the amount they handed over, and the difference is unexplained. On a reversal row the same line is labelled "Qaytarilgan summa".
- [2026-09-11] `TelegramService.SendDocumentAsync` hard-codes the **.docx** MIME type (it was written for contracts) and we send a `.pdf` through it → **reused unchanged; the file name carries `.pdf` and Telegram clients key off the extension** → `TelegramService.cs` is a shared file and five tasks are running in parallel; a one-parameter fix is written up in `docs/PENDING_WIRING.md` for whoever owns that file next. Worth doing, not worth a merge conflict in the money phase.
- [2026-09-11] Where does `ReceiptDeliveryDto` (the POST response) live, given `Dtos/BillingDtos.cs` is frozen and shared? → **in `ReceiptsController.cs`** → adding a type to the frozen file for one endpoint's response buys a merge conflict with four parallel agents. P1-16 moves it if the frontend needs the shape.
- [2026-09-11] Money formatting on the receipt: `CultureInfo("uz-UZ")`? → **no — an explicit `NumberFormatInfo` (group separator U+00A0, decimal comma) and a hand-written Uzbek month table** → the receipt is a client-facing document; its wording must not change because a container image shipped a different ICU version. Tiyin are printed only when non-zero, since the column is `numeric(14,2)` but real amounts are whole so'm.
- [2026-09-11] `[Produces("application/pdf")]` on the PDF endpoint → **removed** → it constrains the output formatter for the *whole action*, so the 404 branch (a JSON `{ message }`) could not be formatted and the client got `406 Not Acceptable` with an empty body instead of "to'lov topilmadi". Caught by the test, not by review. `File(pdf, "application/pdf")` sets the success content type by itself; `[ProducesResponseType]` documents the rest.

## Phase 1 — P1-26, money-flow ring (2026-09-11)

- [2026-09-11] `docs/TASKS.md` stops at P1-25; P1-26 has no written spec section there → **built from the brief as given, no TASKS.md edit** → `TASKS.md` is the plan document for the 25 planned tasks and is referenced by other agents working in parallel; adding a 26th section to it mid-flight would collide with whoever owns that file next. The full rationale lives in the code comments and in this file.
- [2026-09-11] Which accounts become ring nodes? → **only `revenue:*` (incoming) and `expense:*` (outgoing); `cash`, `bank` and `receivable` are excluded** → they are the second leg of every double entry, so including them would draw the same money twice and make the "incoming = centre = outgoing" check meaningless. They answer *where the money sits*, not *where it came from and went*.
- [2026-09-11] What keeps incoming = centre = outgoing exact when income ≠ expense? → **a `net` node: surplus becomes an outgoing link `hub → net`, a deficit becomes an incoming link `net → hub`** → this is the Alphabet Sankey's "net income" column. Putting a deficit on the outgoing side instead would make the outflow exceed the centre and leave "where did it come from" unanswered. The hub value is *summed from the links*, never recomputed, so the equality is structural rather than arithmetic luck; `MoneyFlowTests` asserts it with `decimal` equality (no tolerance).
- [2026-09-11] An account whose net movement over the period is negative (a reversal landing inside the window while the original fell outside) → **kept as a node with its negative value, not dropped or clamped** → dropping it would silently break the balance; the renderer sizes geometry by `Math.abs` and the hover readout shows the signed figure.
- [2026-09-11] `MoneyFlowQueries` as a DI service or a static class? → **static, controller injects the already-registered `IAppDbContext`** → it is a pure read query with no state; this way the endpoint works today without touching `Program.cs`, which belongs to P1-15. Nothing is pending in `docs/PENDING_WIRING.md` for it.
- [2026-09-11] `@types/three` pulls five type-only transitive packages and lags `three` by one release → **pin both to r185 (`three@^0.185.1`, `@types/three@^0.185.4`)** → three.js ships no `.d.ts` of its own, and three has no semver guarantee between minor releases, so a types/runtime version split is a real risk of wrong types rather than a cosmetic mismatch. All five extras are `devDependencies` and never reach the bundle.
- [2026-09-11] Labels: HTML overlay projected each frame, or in-scene sprites? → **canvas-texture sprites with `sizeAttenuation: false`** → an overlay would need a per-frame inline `style.transform`, which the project's UI rules forbid; the flag keeps the text one constant on-screen size, without which the near arc's labels are unreadably large and the far arc's unreadably small while the scene turns.
- [2026-09-11] Hover readout: a tooltip following the cursor, or a fixed panel? → **fixed panel in the canvas's bottom-left** → a cursor-following tooltip needs an inline `style` for positioning. The panel names the node, so nothing is ambiguous, and the hovered object is additionally brightened in 3D.

## Phase 1 — expense entry (`ExpenseService` + `ExpensesController`, 2026-09-11)

- [2026-09-11] **Storno: a second `expenses` row, or a ledger-only reversal?** → **ledger-only: `LedgerService.ReverseAsync`, and `expenses` is not touched at all** → three reasons, any one of which decides it. (1) `Expense` has no `reversal_of` column and the entity is frozen (P1-04), so a second row could only be tied to its original through note *text* — a guess, not a link. (2) `ck_expenses_amount` forbids a negative amount, so the storno row would be positive and **double** the expense in any report that sums `expenses` instead of the ledger — silently, and in the wrong direction. (3) `ledger_entries` is append-only at the database level (`app_rw` has no UPDATE/DELETE); `expenses` is not. The evidence of a reversal belongs in the stronger layer. Consequence: an expense's state (`pending` / `posted` / `reversed`) is **derived from the ledger on every read**, never stored — one source of truth, the one SPEC §3.7 names.
- [2026-09-11] **Expense category — free text (as the entity comment says) or a closed list?** → **closed list of six: `salary, utilities, supplies, rent, repair, other`** → the category becomes a ledger *account* (`expense:<category>`), and accounts are a closed list by design (`Accounts.cs`). Free text means a mistyped "utilites" lands in a report line nobody looks at, with no error anywhere. The list is the client's own answer (docs/TASKS.md §8 Q16) plus `repair`, which P1-26's money-flow ring already draws. Adding a category is one line in `Accounts.cs` — the same deliberate friction as adding an account.
- [2026-09-11] **Four new ledger accounts (`expense:utilities|supplies|rent|repair`) — is growing the frozen closed list acceptable?** → **yes, and `LedgerServiceTests.Hisoblar_royxati_…` was updated to the new list (not weakened, not deleted)** → the alternative was mapping every non-salary expense to `expense:other`, which makes P&L unable to answer "where did it go" — the whole reason the module exists. SPEC §3.7 shows the account list with a trailing "…", i.e. illustrative; the P1-07 acceptance criterion that named ten accounts is satisfied by all ten still being there.
- [2026-09-11] **Payment method for an expense that is waiting for approval — where is it kept?** → **nowhere; the approver names it in `POST /{id}/approve`** → `expenses` has no method column and the entity is frozen, and the method only exists in the ledger (as the credit account, `cash` or `bank`), which is written at approval time. It reads correctly too: the person who authorises the payout is the person who says which account it leaves. The create request still validates `method`, so a bad value fails at the form, not two days later in the director's queue.
- [2026-09-11] **`POST /{id}/approve` was not in the task's list of endpoints (GET, POST, POST /reverse) — add it anyway?** → **yes** → without it an above-threshold expense could never reach the ledger and would sit in the queue forever, which makes SPEC §4.5 decorative. It is also clearly the intended design: `FinanceAction.ApproveExpense` ("chiqimni tasdiqlash — yaratuvchidan boshqa shaxs") has been in the frozen P1-06 matrix from the start. It is a `POST`, so the "no `HttpPut`/`HttpDelete`" criterion is untouched.
- [2026-09-11] **Which `FinanceAction` guards the reversal — a new `ReverseExpense`, or the existing `ApproveExpense`?** → **`ApproveExpense` (director only)** → `FinanceAction` is frozen (P1-06) and `CashierRoleTests` enumerates it row by row; adding a member means editing another task's frozen file and its test. Both actions mean the same thing under §4.5 — a second person signs for money — so reusing it keeps permissions with exactly one source. Written up in `docs/PENDING_WIRING.md` in case the school wants "any second admin may reverse".
- [2026-09-11] **Threshold storage — `BillingSettings` column or a constant?** → **`billing_settings.expense_approval_threshold numeric(14,2) not null default 5000000`, new migration, no new table** → SPEC §4.5 says "configurable threshold", and the client's figure (docs/TASKS.md §8 Q16) is 5 000 000 so'm. Because no table is created, `deploy/init-roles.sql` does **not** have to be re-run: the column inherits `billing_settings`' existing grants. `approved_by <> created_by` stays where it already was — a check constraint (`ck_expenses_approver_differs`, P1-05), so lowering the threshold to 0 still cannot let one person sign for themselves.
- [2026-09-11] The threshold is deliberately **not** added to the frozen `BillingSettingsDto` / `UpdateBillingSettingsRequest` → adding it there changes a contract five parallel agents read, for a settings screen that does not exist yet. Until it does, changing the threshold is an `UPDATE`, exactly like `payment_due_day` was before its screen existed.
- [2026-09-11] **`ReverseAsync` opens no explicit transaction, unlike every other money path here** → **deliberate: that path writes nothing but `LedgerService.ReverseAsync`'s single `SaveChanges`, so it is already atomic** → a transaction around one save is noise that suggests a second write exists. `CreateAsync` and `ApproveAsync` *do* open one, because each writes `expenses` and then the ledger.
- [2026-09-11] **No `audit_log` row is written** → **P1-14's acceptance criterion names "the expense path" explicitly, and `PaymentService` set the precedent of leaving it out** → writing it here would mean two places deciding what an audit row looks like for money. The one rule P1-14 must keep is recorded in `docs/PENDING_WIRING.md`: the row goes *inside* the transaction, before `CommitAsync`.
- [2026-09-11] **The task asked to verify `GET /api/admin/finance/money-flow`; that endpoint does not exist** (it is P1-26, still unbuilt) → **the test asserts the invariant that endpoint must satisfy, directly on `ledger_entries`**: for one accounting day, `Σ expense:* debits == Σ (cash+bank) credits`, `Σ all debits == Σ all credits`, and the whole-ledger trial balance stays balanced. When P1-26 lands, its numbers come from exactly these sums.
- [2026-09-11] **`EnableRetryOnFailure` is enabled in `Program.cs`, and EF refuses user-initiated transactions under a retrying strategy** → **left untouched, reported as a blocker** → the fix is one shared decision (drop the retry option, or route every money operation through `CreateExecutionStrategy()`), it affects payments, invoices and shifts as much as expenses, and it must not be solved four different ways. Verified empirically; full reproduction and both options are in `docs/PENDING_WIRING.md`. The test harness hides it because `ApiFactory` re-registers the DbContext without the retry option.

## Phase 1 — P1-18, director finance dashboard (2026-09-12)

- [2026-09-12] SPEC §4.6 wants the unresolved-variance counter to be **resolvable with a written reason**, but the only endpoint that could persist a resolution (`/api/admin/finance/flags`, P1-14) does not exist yet — verified against the running stack, it answers **404**. → **the counter is built from `GET /api/cash/shifts?onlyWithVariance=true` (live today, `variance` is a generated column nobody can edit), and the "Sabab yozib hal qilish" control is rendered only when the flags endpoint actually answers** → the two wrong alternatives were both worse: a button that pretends to resolve (the director thinks it is handled, the server never heard about it), or a counter that silently shows 0 because the scanner is not running. The screen states which of the two sources it is using, in Uzbek, in place.
- [2026-09-12] `getFinanceFlags()` returns a discriminated result (`{available:true, flags}` / `{available:false, reason}`) instead of an array → returning `[]` on 404 would make "no anomalies" and "the anomaly scanner is not deployed" indistinguishable, which is the exact confusion this module exists to prevent. When P1-14 ships, the counter switches over on its own with no UI change.
- [2026-09-12] The five new tabs are gated on **role** (`admin`, `superadmin`), not on the `finance` permission key → `/api/admin/finance/*` and the cross-cashier shift list are `FinanceAction.ViewBillingReports` / `ViewVarianceReport`, i.e. admin + director only (SPEC §4.3). A `staff` user with the `finance` permission can still open the legacy tabs but would get 403 from every new endpoint, so the tabs are not drawn for them at all.
- [2026-09-12] New reports were added as **tabs inside the existing `/admin/finance` route**, not as new routes → P1-20 owns `App.tsx` and `navigation.ts`, and the phase rule forbids parallel tasks touching them. Nothing is left unwired: the tabs are reachable the moment the page loads.
- [2026-09-12] Cash Flow "Jami (naqd + bank)" monthly rows are summed in the browser → the endpoint gives per-account monthly series and period totals, but no combined monthly series. Period totals (opening / inflow / outflow / closing) still come from the server's root fields, so the headline figures are never client arithmetic; only the chart's combined line is.
- [2026-09-12] Debtors tab defaults to `includeArchived=true` → `DebtorReportQuery` documents the intent ("a departed student's debt is still debt"); defaulting the checkbox off would understate total debt on first load and nobody would notice.

## Phase 1 — P1-19, student / parent finance view (2026-09-12)

- [2026-09-12] The acceptance criteria need invoice data (amount, discount, paid, remaining per category per month) but **no HTTP endpoint exposes it**: `IInvoiceService.ForStudentAsync` exists and is tested, yet no controller calls it (`GET /api/admin/billing/invoices` and `/api/admin/billing/students/{id}` both answer 404 today, verified against the running stack). → **a new read-only controller, `SchoolLms.Server/Controllers/PortalFinanceController.cs`** → the alternative was shipping a page that can only ever render its error state. No existing file was touched: no `Program.cs`, no `BillingDtos.cs`, no `StudentPortalController.cs`.
- [2026-09-12] Should the parent screen call the frozen `billing.ts` / `payments.ts` clients? → **no; a new `src/api/services/portalFinance.ts`** → every path in those two files sits under `/admin/billing/*` or `/cash/*`, i.e. behind `Roles.FinanceStaff` or `FinanceAction.AcceptPayment`. A parent gets 403 there, and even with access the payloads carry cashier id, shift id and invoice ids — things the acceptance criteria forbid on screen. The two frozen files were not modified (four agents depend on them).
- [2026-09-12] `GET /api/receipts/{id}.pdf` is gated by `FinanceAction.AcceptPayment` (cashier + admin + director), so a parent cannot open the receipt that SPEC §4.7 says is *theirs*. → **`GET /api/student/receipts/{paymentId}.pdf`, same `ReceiptService`, ownership check instead of a role check** → the parent holds the identical document the cashier printed; a payment belonging to someone else returns **404, not 403** (the existence of a stranger's receipt is itself information).
- [2026-09-12] `PortalFinanceController` builds `InvoiceService` / `ReceiptService` itself from the request-scoped `AppDbContext` instead of taking them from DI. → **deliberate, copying `FinanceReportsController`** (`docs/PENDING_WIRING.md` §3a) → `Program.cs` is P1-15's shared file and the billing services are still unregistered there; constructing the stateless services locally makes these two endpoints live now and costs nothing later — P1-15 can switch the two properties to constructor parameters in one edit.
- [2026-09-12] Month subtotals: sum them in React, or on the server? → **on the server, in `decimal`** → `types/index.ts` states the rule plainly ("PULNI FRONTEND HISOBLAMAYDI"), and the portal response is a new shape anyway, so there was no reason to make the browser add money. `FinanceView.tsx` contains no arithmetic on money at all — only comparisons with zero.
- [2026-09-12] Does the storno counter-row appear in the parent's payment list as its own entry? → **no — it is nested inside the payment it cancels (`reversal`), and the cancelled payment itself stays visible, struck through, with the reason beside it** → showing both as siblings makes one 2 250 000 payment look like two. The acceptance criterion is that the *reversed payment* is not hidden, and it is not.
- [2026-09-12] The category badge reads `invoices.status`, which is maintained separately from `remaining`. → **if the two disagree, the badge follows the money** (`remaining > 0` never renders "To'langan") → the failure mode is a parent seeing a green "paid" label next to a red 1 800 000 balance and deciding not to pay. Costs one comparison; the underlying `status` is still what the server sent.
- [2026-09-12] The legacy "Balans" badge in `StudentDetailPage.tsx` showed `students.balance` — one number for everything. → **removed and replaced by the new `Moliya` section (the same `FinanceView` the parent sees)** → a single "−1 800 000" cannot say which month or which category, which is precisely what P1-19 exists to fix; and `students.balance` is deleted in P1-21, so anything reading it is already on borrowed time.
- [2026-09-12] `FinanceView` resolves the parent's child by matching the login (phone) against `students.parent_phone`, duplicating `StudentPortalController.TargetAsync`. → **duplicated rather than extracted** → extracting means editing a 1 300-line shared controller while three other agents are in flight. Both copies must move to one helper when SPEC §3.2's guardian many-to-many lands; recorded in `docs/PENDING_WIRING.md`.

## Phase 1.F — P1-17, admin billing catalog UI (2026-09-12)

- [2026-09-12] `api/services/billing.ts` was handed over as "ready", but every function in it still throws `notImplemented(...)` — it is the frozen P1-06 contract, and opening it is a Phase 1.C job on a file three parallel agents are holding → **wrote a second, live client, `api/services/billingCatalog.ts`, and left `billing.ts` untouched** → the 13 `BillingCatalogController` endpoints answer today (verified against the local stack: 5 categories, 18 subscriptions), so building four screens on top of a stub that always rejects would have produced four permanently broken pages. Signatures deliberately mirror `billing.ts`, so when 1.C un-stubs it the pages move over by changing one import line.
- [2026-09-12] `ExpensesController` does not exist — `/api/admin/billing/expenses` returns `404 {"message":"API endpoint topilmadi"}`, although the `expenses` table holds 5 rows → **`ExpensesPage` calls the real URLs anyway and renders a separate "hali serverga ulanmagan" state for a bare 404** → the alternative (an empty array) is the "screen that looks like it works" failure the P1-06 contract note warns about: it passes review and is found in production. The contract for the four endpoints is written up in `docs/PENDING_WIRING.md` §15, and the page needs no change when they land.
- [2026-09-12] SPEC §4.5 requires a second approver for an expense "above N so'm" but never fixes N, and no endpoint exposes it → **`EXPENSE_APPROVAL_THRESHOLD = 5_000_000` as a frontend constant** → the seed data is unambiguous: the 4 600 000 row is unapproved and every row from 7 400 000 up is approved. Kept in one exported constant so that turning it into a school setting later is a one-line deletion, not a search across four files.
- [2026-09-12] `DiscountDto` and `ExpenseDto` expose `CreatedByName` but no `CreatedById`, so the UI cannot compare the creator to the logged-in user by id — and P1-17 must hide the approve button from the creator → **compare normalised full names, and declare an optional `createdById` that takes precedence the moment the backend sends it** → the comparison fails *closed*: two staff with the same name hide the button from each other instead of showing it to the wrong person, and the server's `self_approval` guard is untouched (verified live: `POST .../approve` by the creator returns `403 self_approval`). Adding `CreatedById` to the DTOs is explicitly non-breaking per `BillingDtos.cs`, and the frontend needs no edit when it arrives.
- [2026-09-12] The expense approval queue: fetch a dedicated `/expenses/pending`, mirroring `/discounts/pending`? → **no — derive it in the page from the list already fetched** → `/discounts/pending` exists and is used; inventing a second unbuilt endpoint would have enlarged P1-13's contract for no gain, because "amount above threshold and not yet approved" is decidable from rows the page already holds. One function, `expenseState()`, owns that derivation.
- [2026-09-12] An expense also needs a *reject* action, symmetric with discounts? → **no: approve or storno, nothing else** → a discount is a *request* about future money, so it can be denied. An expense is a fact — the money is already gone — so "reject" would be a lie about the past. SPEC §4.1 gives exactly one correction path, a reversal with a reason, and that is what the page offers. There is no delete control anywhere on the page.
- [2026-09-12] A per-student "monthly total" column on the subscriptions page would be genuinely useful → **not added** → rule 2 at the top of `BillingDtos.cs`: the frontend does no money arithmetic, the server returns computed figures. No endpoint returns that sum today; `reduce`-ing it in JavaScript is how tiyin drift starts. The card shows the subscription count instead, and the real total belongs to the student billing card (P1-09).
- [2026-09-12] Fee categories have no delete endpoint, and the pages needed a consistent story for "remove" → **categories are deactivated, subscriptions are ended with a date, expenses are reversed; nothing in this module deletes** → each of the three has invoices, ledger rows or history hanging off it. The pages say so in plain Uzbek under each table, so the missing button reads as a decision rather than an omission.
- [2026-09-12] Which roles reach these four pages, given `ProtectedRoute role="admin"` also lets `staff` through? → **the pages guard themselves with `admin` + `superadmin` only, and the nav entries recommended to P1-20 carry `roles: ['admin','superadmin']`** → `Roles.FinanceStaff` is `admin,superadmin`, so a `staff` user with the `finance` permission would otherwise see four menu items that answer 403. `cashier` never reaches them twice over: its own nav list, and `BillingGuard` naming the rule explicitly.

## Phase 1 — P1-16, cashier workspace (2026-09-12)
