- [2026-09-12] A cashier cannot search for a student: `GET /api/admin/students` sits behind `[AdminPerm("students")]` and returns **403** for `cashier` (measured on the live stack), and there is no other student lookup a cash-desk role may call. → **added a new, deliberately minimal `GET /api/cash/students?q=` (`CashierStudentsController.cs`), guarded by the existing `[FinanceRole(FinanceAction.AcceptPayment)]`** → the alternative was to admit `cashier` to the admin gate, which would also hand them student create/update/archive and the login-password export. The new endpoint returns five identity fields and nothing else, caps at 25 rows, needs ≥2 characters and skips archived students. Measured: cashier 200, admin 200, staff 403, anonymous 401.
- [2026-09-12] The search endpoint deliberately does **not** return a balance or debt figure → **the only source of "how much does this student owe" stays `GET /api/cash/payments/suggest-allocation`** → that query already encodes the non-obvious rule (a storno row allocates nothing, and a reversed payment's allocations do not count). A second debt formula in a search endpoint would agree on the day it is written and drift on the day one of them is fixed.
- [2026-09-12] Listing a student's open invoices before any amount is typed: `suggest-allocation` returns `[]` when `amount <= 0`, and no other endpoint gives a cashier per-invoice remainders. → **call it with a probe amount of `0.01` and read only `remaining`** → the response already contains every invoice with a positive remainder; the probe only decides which row gets a one-tiyin `suggested`, and the UI ignores `suggested` until the cashier has entered a real amount. The alternative — a new "open invoices for this student" endpoint — would be a third place that computes a remainder.
- [2026-09-12] Where FIFO lives → **entirely on the server; the split editor renders `suggested` and never recomputes it** → TASKS.md P1-16 says "the existing StudentLedger FIFO logic, ported to TS", but `PaymentService.SuggestAllocationAsync` already implements it and is the same code the accrual and debtor reports agree with. Porting it would duplicate a money formula across two languages. Verified across two categories: 1 200 000 so'm fills August tuition (1 000 000) first, then September bus (200 000).
- [2026-09-12] The remainder counter "never goes negative" — enforce by validation-after-the-fact, or by making the state unreachable? → **unreachable: every allocation input is clamped on entry to `min(invoice remaining, payment amount − sum of the other lines)`** → a red "over-allocated" warning would be a state the cashier can sit in while the confirm button is disabled, and the acceptance criterion is about the counter, not about the button. Server-side both limits are still enforced (`allocation_exceeds_amount`, `allocation_exceeds_invoice`, plus the `payment_allocations_total` trigger) — measured 400 for both.
- [2026-09-12] An allocation row cannot be removed, only zeroed → **rows with amount 0 are dropped from the request** → the split editor must contain no control that reads as "remove"; the acceptance criterion for this page is a `grep -i "delete\|o'chir"` that finds nothing outside a comment. Zeroing is also the truthful model: the row is a proposal, not a record.
- [2026-09-12] `cashTotal` is present on an **open** shift's DTO (measured 1 200 000 before close), so the shift bar could show the cashier what the drawer should hold → **not rendered; the open shift shows only the open time and the receipt count** → SPEC §4.2's close discipline exists so the count is made without knowing the expectation. `expectedCash` and `variance` are null until the close response, and the UI must not defeat that from the other side. No payment history or day total is shown on this screen either, for the same reason.
- [2026-09-12] The close dialog is two-phase (enter count → submit → see expected/counted/variance) rather than one screen → **security requirement, not ergonomics** → measured end to end on a throwaway stack: before close `expectedCash: null, variance: null`; a close with no `countedCash` → 400 `invalid_counted_cash`; a close counting 1 240 000 against an expected 1 250 000 → `variance: -10000` returned only in that response.
- [2026-09-12] `payments.ts` / `cashShifts.ts` (frozen in P1-06) are still `notImplemented(...)` stubs pointing at guessed paths, and belong to other Phase 1.F agents running in parallel → **P1-16 wrote its own `src/api/services/cashier.ts` against the real routes instead of editing them** → touching a file another agent owns during a parallel phase buys a merge conflict in the money module. Signatures were kept identical so consolidation later is mechanical; the three non-obvious details are written up in `docs/PENDING_WIRING.md` §16.
- [2026-09-12] The receipt PDF cannot be a plain `<a href>` — the endpoint needs the JWT and the browser will not send it → **the blob is fetched when the receipt opens and the link points at an object URL** → doing it in the click handler works too, but the `await` before `window.open` is what pop-up blockers stop. Prefetching also gives the PDF its own loading and error states instead of a dead button.
- [2026-09-12] Two extra files beyond the four TASKS.md names (`format.ts`, `MoneyInput.tsx`) → **money formatting and money entry are used by all four screens** → the same sum is displayed in three places and entered in two, and "0 entered" must stay distinguishable from "nothing entered" (the close button depends on exactly that difference). One copy, one behaviour.
- [2026-09-12] P1-20 requires `adminPermissions` to gain `billing` and `cashShifts` keys → not added → the billing catalogue, expense and cash-shift endpoints are gated by **role** (`[Authorize(Roles = Roles.FinanceStaff)]`, SPEC §4.3), not by the staff permission list. A checkbox in "Xodimlar va rollar" that grants nothing would be a lie to the director; the criterion predates the §4.3 role decision. Nav entries use `roles: ['admin','superadmin']` instead.

## Phase 1 — P1-23, billing arithmetic unit tests (2026-09-12)

- [2026-09-12] Where do the three new test files live, given every existing test class sits flat in `SchoolLms.Tests` with namespace `SchoolLms.Tests`? → **folder `SchoolLms.Tests/Billing/`, namespace `SchoolLms.Tests.Billing`** → TASKS.md P1-23 names the paths literally (`SchoolLms.Tests/Billing/DiscountMathTests.cs`), and a folder whose namespace does not match it would make `--filter` unreadable. Side effect worth knowing: `--filter FullyQualifiedName~SchoolLms.Tests.Billing` is a substring match, so it also selects `BillingCatalogTests`; use `~SchoolLms.Tests.Billing.` (trailing dot) to get only the new files.
- [2026-09-12] Discount expectations: cross-check the implementations against each other (what `BillingCatalogTests` already does), or hard-code the expected number? → **both, in one table, for all THREE implementations** (`TuitionService.ChargeFor`, `DiscountMath.ChargeFor`, `DiscountService.ChargeFor`) → an "old == new" comparison stays green when both sides are wrong in the same way, and it never looks at `DiscountMath` — which is the copy the accrual job actually calls. Every row now pins the number, so a drift names the guilty implementation.
- [2026-09-12] The rounding rows (`333.33 × 50% → 166.66`, `333.35 × 50% → 166.68`) look inconsistent until you know why → **they pin `decimal.Round(x, 2)` default `MidpointRounding.ToEven`, which is the legacy behaviour being preserved** → the port must not silently become "always round half up"; on a 1 500-student school that is a real number of tiyin per month, and nobody would ever notice it in review.
- [2026-09-12] `AllocationTests` creates invoices through subscriptions + `InvoiceService.AccrueMonthAsync`, not `db.Invoices.Add(...)` → a hand-inserted invoice has no ledger pair, and then "how much does this student owe" could not be cross-checked against `receivable` — the test would be checking allocation bookkeeping while the money question stayed unasked. Cost: three subscriptions with non-overlapping periods to get three different monthly prices.
- [2026-09-12] Each test in `AllocationTests`/`AccrualTests` takes its own throwaway database (like `InvoiceServiceTests`), which exhausted PostgreSQL's `max_connections` (`53300`) and knocked over five *existing* tests → **each class clears its own pool in `DisposeAsync` (`NpgsqlConnection.ClearPool`)**, not `ClearAllPools()` → the fixture's per-database connection strings each cache their own pool and nothing ever closed them; `ClearAllPools()` would also drop the running application's pool. Alternative (sharing one database) was rejected: the ledger-balance assertions are database-wide by nature.
- [2026-09-12] The 200-operation randomised ledger scenario needs a reproducible RNG → **fixed seed `20260912`, overridable with the `BILLING_LEDGER_SEED` environment variable, and the effective seed is printed in every assertion message together with the operation counts and the last five operations** → "it failed once on CI" is worthless without the seed; a fully random seed would give an intermittently red suite that nobody can reproduce.
- [2026-09-12] In that scenario, the expected `receivable` balance is computed from the raw tables (`invoices`, `payment_allocations`, `payments`), not from `StudentBillingDto` → the DTO path has a live defect (see below), and the ledger invariant must not depend on a reporting-layer bug. The DTO field itself is asserted separately in `AllocationTests`.
- [2026-09-12] **`AllocationTests.Ortiqcha_tolov_taqsimlanmagan_avans_bolib_qoladi` is left RED on purpose.** `InvoiceService.PaymentsForStudentAsync` fills `PaymentDto.ReversedBy` with `Dictionary<Guid,Guid>.GetValueOrDefault(p.Id)`, which yields `Guid.Empty` (not `null`) for a payment that was never reversed. `ForStudentAsync` then filters on `p.ReversedBy is null`, so `StudentBillingDto.Credit` is **always 0** — an advance never appears on the student's card. `PaymentService.ToDtosAsync` does the same lookup correctly (`TryGetValue(...) ? storno : null`). Per the P1-23 brief the expectation was **not** adjusted to the code; the fix belongs to `qa-debug`.

## Phase 1 — P1-22, financial security suite (2026-09-12)

- [2026-09-12] `app_rw` does not exist during the test run, so the migration's `GRANT`/`REVOKE` block skips itself and the whole of SPEC §4.1 stays untested (`docs/TESTING.md` §4, `docs/PENDING_WIRING.md` §6) → **`PostgresFixture` now creates a bare `app_rw` *before* `MigrateAsync()` and gives it only the baseline privileges of `deploy/init-roles.sql` step 4 (full CRUD on every table, `payments` DELETE included) plus `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE schoollms_owner`; it writes **no** financial `REVOKE`** → the migration is `NOCREATEROLE` by design in production too, so something outside it must create the role — in production that is `init-roles.sql`, here the fixture. The split is the point: the fixture hands the role the worst case and `Migrations/Sql/billing_guards.sql` has to take `UPDATE`/`DELETE` back, so the tests measure the migration's grants, not the fixture's. Verified by mutation: deleting the `REVOKE` line turns all six immutability assertions red.
- [2026-09-12] §4.3 row 8 "Record an expense" has no endpoint in the new stack (`POST /admin/billing/expenses` is still `notImplemented(..., 'P1-13')` in the frozen client contract) → **the three cells assert 404 plus "no `expenses` row was written", and the ⛔ half is reported as proven while the two ✅ cells are reported as not proven** → asserting a made-up 201 would be a test that passes against code that does not exist. The 404 expectation deliberately goes red the day the endpoint lands, which forces whoever adds it to write the real RBAC row.
- [2026-09-12] §4.3 row 3 "Edit or delete a payment" — expected status for `PUT`/`DELETE` on `/api/billing/payments/{id}` → **404, not 405** → measured, not assumed: the SPA fallback route claims the path before ASP.NET Core can emit a method-not-allowed. Either code means "no such operation", and 404 additionally leaks nothing about the row existing.
- [2026-09-12] §4.3 row 7 "See variance report across cashiers" has two HTTP surfaces with different shapes — `GET /api/cash/shifts/{id}/z-report` answers 403 `not_your_shift`, while `GET /api/cash/shifts?cashierId=…` answers **200 with the filter silently narrowed to the caller** (P1-10's documented contract) → **the 24-cell matrix uses the z-report (a real 403/200/200), and the list-scoping behaviour is asserted in a separate named test** → collapsing both into one cell would have meant either asserting a 403 that never comes or recording "200" for a ⛔ row.
- [2026-09-12] `POST /api/cash/shifts/open` and `.../close` do not reject a `cashierId` sent in the body — they ignore it, unlike `PaymentsController.RejectServerDerivedFields` → **the test asserts the security outcome that does hold (the shift is opened for the JWT subject, never for the id in the body) and the §4.4 wording gap is reported instead of fixed** → P1-22 is a test task; changing a controller to make a test greener is exactly the move that hides which layer is actually holding.

## Phase 1 — P1-14, billing audit + nightly anomaly scan (2026-09-12)

- [2026-09-12] `audit_log.before`/`after` are `text` (Phase 0 shipped them that way) but SPEC §4.6 says `jsonb` — convert, or leave and serialise? → **convert to `jsonb` in the P1-14 migration** → the audit trail exists to answer questions like "who moved more than 500 000", and `LIKE '%Amount%'` over a text column is not an audit. `jsonb` also rejects malformed JSON at INSERT time, so a broken snapshot never reaches the table. Every current writer already goes through `JsonSerializer`, so no data changes shape.
- [2026-09-12] EF autogenerates `ALTER COLUMN before TYPE jsonb` with no `USING`, which PostgreSQL refuses (`cannot be cast automatically`) → **the two `AlterColumn` calls were replaced by hand with `Migrations/Sql/audit_log_jsonb.sql`** → `MigrationBuilder.AlterColumn` cannot express `USING`, so the generated migration would have failed on any non-empty database. The SQL file first makes old rows safe (empty string → NULL, non-JSON → JSON string via PG16's `IS JSON`), then changes the type. Nothing is deleted.
- [2026-09-12] Where does the actor name on a finance audit row come from, given `AuditService.Record` reads `HttpContext` and the scan/accrual jobs have none? → **new static `AuditService.Entry(...)` that takes the actor as a parameter, plus a per-instance `ActorNames` cache** → SPEC §4.4 already makes the actor an explicit argument of every finance service, and the background jobs would otherwise log every ledger batch as "Tizim". The cache exists because the monthly accrual calls `LedgerService.PostAsync` once per invoice — 500 students would mean 500 identical `SELECT full_name` queries for the audit row alone.
- [2026-09-12] The ledger audit snapshot cannot contain `ledger_entries.id` (bigint identity, only known after `SaveChanges`) → **snapshot without ids, one `SaveChanges` for money + audit** → the alternative (save, then add the audit row, then save again) puts the audit in a second commit; a crash in between leaves money with no audit trail. The batch is still uniquely addressable by `ref_type` + `ref_id`.
- [2026-09-12] "Outside the cashier's normal hours" (SPEC §4.6) has no schema behind it — `users` has no working-hours column → **fixed 08:00–20:00 Tashkent window in `AnomalySettings`** → adding a column to `users` collides with P1-21, and a per-cashier schedule is a feature nobody asked for. The window is deliberately wide: the target is a payment written at 03:00, not a cashier who stayed late. Moving it to `billing_settings` is two columns and one migration when someone actually needs it.
- [2026-09-12] How far back does the nightly scan look? → **90 days for the three event conditions, no limit for "paid without allocations"** → the first three are events frozen at a point in time, so re-reading years of payments every night buys nothing; 90 days still covers the app being down for weeks. The fourth is a *state* — an old invoice can become wrongly `paid` today — and its query is a highly selective anti-join, so it is cheap to run unbounded.
- [2026-09-12] An invoice with `amount - discount = 0` is `paid` with no allocations by design (`InvoiceService.StatusFor`) → **excluded from the "paid without allocations" condition** → without that line the scan would raise dozens of false flags every month for 100 %-discount and free-tuition students, and a dashboard counter nobody believes is worse than no counter.
- [2026-09-12] Flag duplication across nightly runs → **unique index `(kind, ref_id)`, not an application-side "have I seen this" check** → the scan re-reads the same window every night; an application check would drift the first time two runs overlap. The service filters known pairs first purely to avoid a pointless round trip, and retries once on 23505 if a concurrent run wins the race.
- [2026-09-12] `app_rw` needs `UPDATE` to close a flag but must never edit or delete one → **table-level `REVOKE UPDATE, DELETE, TRUNCATE`, then column-level `GRANT UPDATE (resolved_at, resolved_by, resolved_reason)`** → the `payments` treatment (full revoke) would have killed the resolve flow, and leaving table-level UPDATE would let the summary or the amount be rewritten later. Order matters and is commented in `anomaly_guards.sql`: revoke first, then grant the three columns.
- [2026-09-12] Where does the flag entity live, given TASKS.md names `SchoolLms.Application/Billing/Anomaly.cs`? → **entity `FinanceAnomalyFlag` in `SchoolLms.Domain/Billing.cs`, DTOs + settings + `IAnomalyService` in `Application/Billing/Anomaly.cs`** → every EF entity in this repository lives in Domain, and its status constants sit beside it (`InvoiceStatus`, `PaymentMethod`, `CashShiftStatus`). Putting an EF-mapped type in the Application layer for one file name would be the only exception in the codebase.
- [2026-09-12] The manual `POST /flags/scan` endpoint calls `IAnomalyService.ScanAsync` directly rather than the hosted `AnomalyScanService.RunOnceAsync` → **the hosted service only adds a DI scope and a log line, both of which a request already has** → resolving a `BackgroundService` from a controller needs the singleton + `AddHostedService(sp => ...)` dance for no gain.

## Phase 1 — P1-24, cash shift and receipt integrity tests (2026-09-12)

- [2026-09-12] `CashShiftServiceTests` (P1-10) already runs 50 parallel `NextReceiptNoAsync` calls, so what is left for P1-24's concurrency criterion? → **run the 50 callers one layer higher: 50 parallel `PaymentService.AcceptAsync`, i.e. the whole money transaction (payment + allocation + invoice status + two ledger rows) under the per-shift advisory lock** → the number allocator can be perfectly correct and the result still be gapless-broken if its *caller* draws the transaction boundary wrong, and P1-11 is the only caller that matters. `PaymentsTests` cannot cover this either: it substitutes a `ShiftDouble` for `ICashShiftService`, so the real lock never runs in that file. The new test wires the real `CashShiftService` + `PaymentService` + `LedgerService` together.
- [2026-09-12] Connection pool for 50 parallel writers → **a dedicated throwaway database (`Postgres.CreateDatabaseAsync`) with `MaxPoolSize = callers + 10`** → the shared fixture string is `MaxPoolSize = 10` on purpose (20+ test classes share one container). With 10 slots the 50 callers queue in the *pool*, never inside a transaction, so the advisory lock is never contended: the test would be green and would prove nothing. The +10 covers the arrange/assert contexts; `postgres:17-alpine` allows 100 connections and the application's own pool lives on a different database and is capped at 10, so the ceiling is not reached.
- [2026-09-12] Those 50 connections then broke **other** test classes with `53300: remaining connection slots are reserved…` — Npgsql keeps them idle-but-open for `ConnectionIdleLifetime` (5 min) after the test ends → **`NpgsqlConnection.ClearAllPools()` in the test class constructor *and* `Dispose`** → clearing on the way *in* matters as much as on the way out: `CashShiftServiceTests` leaves ~55 idle connectors of its own behind and does not clean up, and this task may not modify it. Every test class lives in the single `schoollms` xUnit collection, so they run strictly sequentially and clearing the global pool cannot interrupt anything in flight; `PostgresFixture` already uses the same call. Caught by running the full suite, not by running the new file alone — which is the whole reason to run the full suite.
- [2026-09-12] How does the test prove it is *genuinely* concurrent rather than accidentally sequential? → **it measures the peak number of callers simultaneously inside `AcceptAsync` and asserts `>= 10`** → 50 tasks released by one `TaskCompletionSource` is the *intent*; the peak counter is the *evidence*. If the thread pool, the connection pool or a future refactor serialised them, the exact-`{1..50}` assertion would still pass while testing nothing, and that silent downgrade is exactly what this criterion exists to prevent. Floor is 10, not 50, so a two-core CI box with a slow thread-pool ramp does not turn a real signal into a flake.
- [2026-09-12] Test files sit in `SchoolLms.Tests/Billing/` (the path `docs/TASKS.md` P1-24 names) → **folder namespace `SchoolLms.Tests.Billing`, matching P1-23's files in the same folder** → the `using` directives are compilation-unit level (file-scoped namespace), so `SchoolLms.Application.Billing` still resolves from global scope; nothing else changes.
- [2026-09-12] The `variance` criterion says "an explicit `UPDATE` attempt (as `app_rw`) must fail" — should the test call `TestDatabase.RequireRealAppRw()`? → **no, and the reason is written into the test** → `variance` is `generated always as (counted_cash - expected_cash) stored`, so the `UPDATE` is rejected with SQLSTATE `428C9` for *every* role including the table owner. This guarantee is not grant-based, so gating it on `RequireRealAppRw()` would add ceremony, not protection. The test still connects with `Database.AppRwConnectionString` (the request path's own role — a real `app_rw` since P1-22) and adds a **control assertion** that the same connection *can* update an ordinary column of `cash_shifts` — without it, "app_rw has no rights on `cash_shifts` at all" would make the test pass for entirely the wrong reason. The grant-based half (`payments`, `payment_allocations`, `ledger_entries`) is P1-22's.
- [2026-09-12] Should the HTTP tests boot their own `WithWebHostBuilder` host with the billing services registered, the way `CashShiftServiceTests` and `PaymentsTests` do? → **no — they use the plain `ApiFixture` host, i.e. the application's own DI graph** → that pattern existed only because P1-15 had not wired `Program.cs` yet; it now has (`ICashShiftService`, `IPaymentService`, `ILedgerService` are all registered). Re-registering them in the test would hide the real wiring: delete the `AddScoped<IPaymentService, …>` line from `Program.cs` and a self-wiring test stays green while production returns 500. Now that regression shows up here immediately. (The two files' first draft did boot their own host; it was removed after merging master.)
- [2026-09-12] Falsifiability was verified, not assumed: `CashShiftService.LockShiftAsync` was temporarily replaced with `Task.CompletedTask`, and both concurrency tests went red (`PaymentException: Chek raqami band…`, i.e. the `ix_payments_cash_shift_id_receipt_no` unique index catching the duplicate) while the other three receipt tests stayed green — correctly, since they do not depend on the lock. The sabotage was reverted immediately and is not committed.
- [2026-09-12] The Z-report criterion is asserted **two ways in the same test** — against hand-written expected amounts *and* against a grouping computed independently from `payments` → the DB-derived check alone could agree with the report because both sides repeat the same mistake; the hand-written numbers are the real protection and the derived one proves the report is not reading a stale cache.
- [2026-09-12] Two defects were confirmed with a throwaway probe while writing these tests, and neither is asserted by a committed test → **reported to the orchestrator instead; the probe was deleted** → a red test would break the "0 failed" gate this task must hold, and a test that pins the *current* behaviour would make the defect permanent. (1) The retrying execution strategy vs. user-initiated transactions — measured as `InvalidOperationException: The configured execution strategy 'NpgsqlRetryingExecutionStrategy' does not support user-initiated transactions` at base commit `cb51655`; **already fixed on master**, where `Program.cs` now omits `EnableRetryOnFailure` on the request-path context on purpose and the comment quotes that exact message. Nothing left to do beyond not regressing it. (2) `CashShiftService.ExpectedCashAsync` joins `ledger.ref_id = payments.id` alone, so a reversal's `credit cash` lands on the **original** payment's shift rather than the approver's — the exact error `docs/PENDING_WIRING.md` §10 warned about. **Still open**; reproduction and measured numbers are in the P1-24 hand-off. Fixing it is `qa-debug`'s call, not a test author's.

## Demo data — teacher account shows a full school (2026-09-12)

- [2026-09-12] Roster size: the client asked for "10 ta namuna" and the seeder read that as 10 students *for the whole school*, all in 1-A → **10 students per class × 10 classes = 100** → nine of the ten homeroom teachers opened onto an empty "Sinf rahbarligi", an empty journal and `progress = 0`. 100 rows is still a demo, not a load test; the per-class count is what makes every teacher account believable.
- [2026-09-12] Generated names for the 90 new students, or 90 hand-written tuples? → **generated from `FAMILIES` + `MALE_GIVEN`/`FEMALE_GIVEN` with a fixed `random.Random(20260910)`** → idempotency here is name-based (`find(..., "fullName")`), so the generator MUST produce the identical list on every run; a fixed seed does that and keeps the data section readable. 1-A keeps its ten hand-written names because the old demo scripts and screenshots reference them.
- [2026-09-12] `TODAY` was frozen at `2026-09-09` → **`min(max(date.today(), 2026-09-09), 2026-10-30)`** → the journal, "bugungi dars jadvali", pickup requests and the canteen are all compared against the *server* clock (`AppClock.Now`), so a frozen seeder date silently drifts out of sync. Clamped to quarter 1 because the whole demo calendar is pinned to the 2026/2027 school year: outside it the journal and progress would have nothing to attach to.
- [2026-09-12] Journal only covers dates up to today (~2 weeks), so `progress` reads ~19%, not ~100% → **left honest** → marking future lessons "o'tildi" would make `ProgressScreen` show 100% in September and would contradict `expectedByToday`, which is what paints the red "Rejadan orqada" banner. Conducted now equals expected-by-today, so every bar is green and the percentage grows on its own as the demo ages.
- [2026-09-12] Assignments and the teacher's own chat message are written while logged in **as each teacher** (10 extra logins) rather than by admin → **accepted, with a 429-aware `demo_login()` retry** → `GET /api/teacher/assignments` filters on `CreatedByUserId`, so an admin-created assignment is invisible in the teacher app; there is no admin endpoint to create one on a teacher's behalf and inventing one is out of scope for a seeder. The login endpoint allows 10/min per IP, so the helper waits for the window instead of failing.
- [2026-09-12] Pickup requests are seeded for **every** class (one student each) even though they expire at midnight → the "Sinf rahbarligi" screen renders the pending/handover state, and an empty one is exactly the complaint being fixed. The seeder is re-run before a demo anyway; the comment in `seed_student_side` says so.
- [2026-09-12] LMS: 1-A keeps all ten subjects, the other nine classes get three each (37 total, not 100) → the "Ta'lim" screen has a class-filter chip row, so it needs material in several classes, but a teacher already sees every class's LMS (they teach in all of them) — tripling the content would have added rows nobody looks at.

## Phase 1 — P1-21, retiring the legacy finance path (2026-09-12)

- [2026-09-12] `students.discount_pct/_amount/_note` are dropped, so the discount fields on the student create/edit form have nowhere to go → **removed from the form, the Excel import template, `StudentPayload`, `StudentDto` and `StudentNotebookDto` — not re-pointed at the `discounts` table** → SPEC §8.1 Q5 says every discount needs the director's approval. A field on the student card that writes an approved discount would be a hole straight through §4.5; a field that writes a *pending* one would look like it worked and silently change nothing. The replacement screen already exists (Moliya → Chegirmalar, P1-17).
- [2026-09-12] `StudentDto.Balance` in contexts that carry no money (journal, attendance, class rating, teacher portal) → **`decimal?`, left `null`, not `0`** → `0` reads as "this student owes nothing", which is a different and wrong statement. `null` says "not computed here". The TS side is `balance?: number` and the two list screens read `s.balance ?? 0`.
- [2026-09-12] `GET /api/admin/students` used to return the `Student` entity, whose `balance` column no longer exists → **it now returns `StudentDto` with a derived balance, filled by ONE bulk `StudentBalanceQuery.ForManyAsync` call** → the alternative (a second request the SPA merges client-side) puts the debt formula on two sides of the wire. The bulk call is two grouped queries regardless of the number of students.
- [2026-09-12] Salary payouts move to `expenses`, but `expenses` has no teacher column and four screens ask "how much did teacher X get in month M" → **added nullable `expenses.teacher_id` (FK → `teachers.id`, RESTRICT) plus `ck_expenses_teacher_only_salary`** → the only alternative was parsing the free-text note, which is a guess, not a relation. The check constraint keeps the column from being filled on a non-salary row, where it would silently distort the salary report.
- [2026-09-12] What counts as "salary paid" now that expenses can be pending or reversed → **only expenses that have a ledger batch and no reversal batch** (`SalaryPaymentQuery`) → an expense row above the approval threshold is money that has *not* left yet (SPEC §4.5), and a reversed one is money that came back. Counting either would understate the teacher's outstanding balance.
- [2026-09-12] `POST /api/admin/students/{id}/payments` → **410 Gone with the replacement path in the body, not deleted outright** → a 404 reads as "wrong URL" and invites a retry; 410 says the route is closed. The old path had no cashier, no shift, no receipt number and the row was editable afterwards — precisely SPEC §4.1/§4.2.
- [2026-09-12] `PUT /api/admin/classes/{id}?applyFee=` and `PUT /api/admin/students/{id}?applyDiscount=` → **both query parameters removed; changing a class fee or a student's class no longer touches money** → the price now lives in `student_subscriptions.monthly_amount`. Keeping the old behaviour would let one click overwrite every agreed subscription price in a class.
- [2026-09-12] `FinanceController` keeps exactly one endpoint (`GET /salary-report`) → the other five had a replacement (`/pnl`, `/cashflow`, `/debtors`, and the write endpoints by the cash desk and `/api/admin/expenses`); the salary report has none. Its source changed to `expenses`, so its "berilgan" column now agrees with P&L instead of contradicting it.
- [2026-09-12] Rollover (`POST /academic-year/rollover`) used to delete every finance row and zero every balance when `clearFinance` was set → **it now closes all open subscriptions and deletes nothing** → `payments`, `payment_allocations` and `ledger_entries` have no DELETE grant for `app_rw` (SPEC §4.1), so the old behaviour would fail with SQLSTATE 42501 — and if it succeeded it would be the fraud the module exists to prevent. Closing subscriptions is the honest reading of "start the new year clean": nothing further is billed at last year's price.
- [2026-09-12] Graduating students are deleted by rollover, but `invoices`/`payments` reference `students(id)` with RESTRICT → **a graduate with any financial history is archived (login blocked, subscriptions closed) instead of deleted; one with none is deleted as before** → otherwise the whole rollover transaction dies on a foreign key at the first paying student. `DELETE /api/admin/students/{id}` gained the same guard, returning 409 with a readable message.
- [2026-09-12] There was no HTTP way to run the accrual — `BillingAccrualService` only ticks at startup and every 12 h, so a freshly seeded database has subscriptions and no invoices → **added `POST /api/admin/billing/accrual/run` (admin/director, idempotent)**, which `tools/seed_demo.py` and `tools/seed_billing.py` both call. The actor is the JWT subject, not the fallback director the background job uses, so the ledger says who pressed the button.
- [2026-09-12] `AuditService.Snapshot(FinanceTransaction)` could not compile once the entity was gone, and `AuditService.cs` belongs to another agent this phase → **the single overload was deleted and the change flagged in `docs/PENDING_WIRING.md`; `EntityFinanceTransaction` stays** so the audit screen can still filter rows written before today.
- [2026-09-12] The acceptance grep `\.Balance` still matches two lines — `service.BalanceAsync(...)` in `LedgerServiceTests` and the same call in `Billing/*Tests` → **not renamed** → `ILedgerService.BalanceAsync` is an *account* balance and lives in `IBillingServices.cs`, which is frozen (P1-06); renaming a frozen member while P1-22/23/24 are writing tests against it buys a merge conflict in the money module to satisfy a regex written to catch a dropped column. Zero hits for the column itself: `grep -rn "students\.balance\|s\.Balance\|student\.Balance"` is empty.

## Phase 3 — Telegram Mini App, parent panel (2026-09-12)

- [2026-09-12] The `/api/tg/...` contract was not in `docs/PENDING_WIRING.md` when the parent panel was built, and no `tg` route exists in the server yet → **every parent screen was built against the existing portal endpoints (`/api/student/*`), behind one file — `src/lib/parentApi.js`** → the task allows this explicitly, and keeping the calls in one module makes the later switch one line per endpoint instead of a search across five screens. Nothing else in `ui-tg` knows an endpoint path.
- [2026-09-12] Every `parentApi` function takes `childId` as its first argument even though the server ignores `?studentId=` for a `parent` (it resolves the child from `users.email` = phone ↔ `students.parent_phone`, deliberately, so a parent cannot type a stranger's id) → **the argument is passed anyway** → when `guardians` / `student_guardians` lands, the screens do not change: they already answer "which child". The cost today is a query parameter the server drops.
- [2026-09-12] How does the panel learn the list of children before `/api/tg/children` exists? → **it asks for it, and falls back to `/api/student/me` (one child) only on 404/405** → falling back on 401 or 500 would hide a real failure, and in the worst case show a parent a child that is not theirs. With one child the switcher renders nothing at all, so the degraded state looks deliberate rather than broken.
- [2026-09-12] The selected child lives in `ParentPanel` (the shell), not in the tabs, and each tab is mounted with `key={child.id}` → **switching the child refetches all five tabs and resets their local UI state (open month, selected week)** → the failure this prevents is silent: a parent reading the older child's grades next to the younger child's debt, with nothing on screen saying so.
- [2026-09-12] "Was the child at school today" — turnstile or journal? → **journal** (`/api/student/attendance` rows filtered to today) → the turnstile endpoint is `/api/admin/students/turnstile/dashboard`, behind `AdminPerm("students")`; a parent gets 403. Consequence: attendance is per lesson, so the card distinguishes three states and never guesses — absent (with the reason), late, and "not marked yet". Showing "at school" for an unmarked day would be a lie the school cannot back up.
- [2026-09-12] Announcements on the home screen = the class chat (`/api/student/chat`), newest three → broadcasts (`/api/admin/messages/broadcast`) are delivered through Telegram/push and are not readable back per student; the class chat is the only announcement feed a parent can actually query today.
- [2026-09-12] Which endpoint feeds the schedule — `/api/student/schedule` or `/api/student/journal`? → **journal** → it returns the same lessons *with the date attached*, plus topic, homework, the child's grade and the absence reason in one request. The date is what lets "today" be highlighted exactly instead of inferred from the device clock and a day index.
- [2026-09-12] `ScheduleMath.GetQuarterWeeks` is duplicated in `screens/parent/weeks.js` → the schedule endpoint is addressed by `?quarter=&week=`, so the client has to know which dates a week number covers or the "next week" arrow cannot say where it is going. Verified against the live server: quarter 1 week 2 = 2026-09-07…2026-09-12, exactly what `/api/student/meta` and `/api/student/journal` report. Both copies carry the two edge rules (quarter starting on a Sunday, week numbers that keep counting past clamped weeks).
- [2026-09-12] Receipt PDF: `window.open` (as the web portal does) or a blob download? → **blob + `<a download>`** → inside the Telegram in-app browser a popup is unreliable and can be swallowed silently; a download hands the file to the client's own document viewer on both iOS and Android. Verified the endpoint itself with curl: `GET /api/student/receipts/{id}.pdf` returns 43 KB of `application/pdf` for the paying parent.
- [2026-09-12] `Stepper` in `components/ui.jsx` gained an optional `disabledPrev` prop (the twin of the existing `disabledNext`) → the quarter and week steppers reach a first element, and a dead arrow that silently does nothing is worse than a visibly disabled one. Additive: the default is `undefined`, so the existing behaviour of every current caller is unchanged.
- [2026-09-12] Canteen dishes for 2026-09-07…09-15 were added to the **local demo database** through the existing admin endpoint (`POST /api/admin/canteen/{date}/{meal}`) → the table was empty, and an "Ovqat" tab with nothing in it tells a school council nothing about the tab. No code and no seed script changed; the days beyond 09-15 stay empty on purpose, so the empty state ("menu not entered yet") is also demonstrable.

## Phase 3 — Telegram Mini App backend: auth, linking, guardians (2026-09-12)

- [2026-09-12] How does a Telegram user prove which school account is theirs — `request_contact` phone, or a code the school issues? → **a one-time code from the admin panel (`POST /api/admin/telegram/link-codes`), 8 characters, 15-minute TTL, single use, stored as SHA-256** → the phone Telegram vouches for is trustworthy, but the *mapping from phone to account* in this database is not: `students.parent_phone` is typed in by hand, is shared between siblings, is often the father's number for a mother's account, and nobody verifies it. "I said a number, I got the account" is exactly the claim the task forbids. A code makes the school the one that authorises the link, and leaves an audit row saying which staff member did it and which Telegram id used it. `request_contact` also happens in the *bot*, not the Mini App, so the Mini App would have to poll for a side-channel event.
- [2026-09-12] `initData` replay window → **24 hours (`TelegramInitData.MaxAge`), plus a 5-minute allowance for a clock that runs fast** → Telegram's own documented recommendation, and anything shorter buys little here: a successful check mints a 12-hour JWT (`JwtOptions.ExpiresHours`), so a captured `initData` is worth at most that regardless. What the window actually prevents is the *unbounded* replay — a screenshot of `initData` from March opening the app in September. One constant to tighten if the client wants it stricter.
- [2026-09-12] Unlinked Telegram account: 401, 403 or 404? → **200 with `status: "unlinked"`** → 401 means "I do not know who you are", and here we know exactly who they are, because Telegram signed it. The two cases need completely different screens ("try again" vs "get a code from the school"), so the frontend needs to branch on something more specific than a status code. A forged `initData` still gets a bare 401 with `code: "invalid_init_data"`.
- [2026-09-12] Only `hash` is removed from the data-check-string; the newer `signature` field stays in → per Telegram's HMAC algorithm. Dropping unknown fields "to be safe" is how the check starts rejecting valid data the day Telegram adds a field.
- [2026-09-12] `initData` values are decoded with `Uri.UnescapeDataString`, not `HttpUtility.UrlDecode` → the HTML-form decoder turns `+` into a space; Telegram encodes with `encodeURIComponent`, where a space is `%20` and `+` is a literal `+`. The wrong decoder corrupts the `user` JSON and rejects a correct signature.
- [2026-09-12] Guardian primary keys: SPEC §3.2 says `uuid`, this schema's `students.id` and `users.id` are `text` → **`text`, like their neighbours** → the foreign keys have to match the referenced column type. A `uuid` PK on `guardians` with `text` FKs to students and users would be a schema that looks deliberate and reads as an accident.
- [2026-09-12] Matching a phone to a guardian: full digits or the last nine? → **last nine, as a `generated always as (right(regexp_replace(phone,'[^0-9]','','g'), 9)) stored` column** → `PhoneUtil.Key` already defines "same phone" that way for the Telegram bot, and "+998 90 123 45 67" must not produce a second guardian beside "901234567". Generated in the database rather than written by the app so the rule cannot drift between C# and the backfill SQL.
- [2026-09-12] `students.parent_phone` stays and keeps its readers → **`GuardianSync` mirrors it into `guardians`/`student_guardians` on student create, update and Excel import; the reverse direction does not exist** → P1-21 has just finished one large retirement and the task explicitly says not to start a second. Without the mirror the new tables would be stale the day after the migration: the backfill only covers rows that already exist. Follow-up recorded in `docs/PENDING_WIRING.md`.
- [2026-09-12] `StudentPortalController.TargetAsync` and `PortalFinanceController.ResolveAsync` still resolve a parent by phone → **left alone; the new `/api/tg/parent/*` surface uses `GuardianAccess` and takes `{studentId}` in the path** → for a one-child family both rules give the same answer, and rewriting the parent path inside a 1 275-line controller while two frontend agents build against it is a bad trade. The child switcher is what the demo needs and it now exists.
- [2026-09-12] Guardian account login → **the digits of their phone number, role `parent`** → the existing parent paths match `users.email` against `students.parent_phone`. Any other login would give a guardian an account that works in the Mini App and fails on the web portal.
- [2026-09-12] Unread class-chat counts needed a read marker and `UserSettings.NotificationsReadAt` is a single timestamp for the whole admin bell → **new `chat_reads (user_id, channel, read_at)` table** → a teacher has one channel per class and reads them separately; one timestamp would mark all of them read at once. The teacher's own messages never count as unread.
- [2026-09-12] The Mini App overview does **not** report an attendance percentage → the rule (`lesson_notes.conducted` × subgroup × late-vs-absent) is already written twice, in `Analytics` and `StudentProfileBuilder`. A third copy on a summary screen is how two screens end up showing different percentages for the same child. The overview carries the raw quarter counts; the full picture is one tap away at `/attendance`.
- [2026-09-12] `SessionFactory` extracted from `AuthController`'s three private methods → the Mini App must issue the *same* token, and two login paths drift: one would block an archived teacher and the other would not. Behaviour is unchanged, including clearing `users.initial_password` on a Telegram login — the account is in use, so holding the plaintext for the superadmin no longer buys anything.
- [2026-09-12] `telegram_link_codes` has no `DELETE` grant for `app_rw` → it is the audit trail for "who authorised this link". The three other new tables are current state, not history, and keep full CRUD.
## Telegram Mini App — o'qituvchi paneli (2026-09-12)

- [2026-09-12] `/api/tg/...` shartnomasi `docs/PENDING_WIRING.md` da hali yo'q edi (`TgController` ham yozilmagan) → **ekranlar mavjud `/api/teacher/...` portaliga qurildi, lekin HAR BIR so'rov `src/lib/teacherApi.js` dagi bitta funksiya ortida** → backend `/api/tg/teacher/...` ni chiqarganda o'sha fayldagi `BASE` bitta qatorda o'zgaradi va ekranlarga tegilmaydi. Boshqa yo'l — shartnomani kutib turish — demo sanasini o'tkazib yuborardi.
- [2026-09-12] "Davomat olindimi" degan savolga bazada alohida maydon yo'q → **dars qaydining `conducted` ("dars o'tildi") belgisi shu ma'noda ishlatiladi** → davomat saqlanganda aynan shu belgi qo'yiladi (`PUT /teacher/journal/notes`), ya'ni ilova o'zi yozadigan holatni o'zi o'qiydi va sinf jurnali bilan bir xil haqiqatni ko'rsatadi. `JournalService.SetEntryAsync` ham baho/sabab kiritilganda shu belgini qo'yadi, ya'ni admin panelidagi hisob bilan ham zid emas.
- [2026-09-12] Davomat saqlanganda mavzu va uyga vazifa yo'qolib ketishi mumkin edi (`SetLessonNoteRequest` ularni ham ustiga yozadi) → **mavjud qayd oldin o'qiladi va `topic`/`homework` o'zgarishsiz qaytariladi**; baho ham shunday (`grade` mavjud yozuvdan olinadi) → davomat ekrani faqat davomatni o'zgartirishi kerak. Tekshirildi: sababni qo'yganda `grade: 3, homework: 1` saqlanib qoldi.
- [2026-09-12] O'quvchi "kelmadi" dan "keldi" ga qaytarilganda katakda boshqa hech narsa qolmasa → **katak `DELETE /teacher/journal` bilan butunlay o'chiriladi** (aks holda hamma maydoni bo'sh yozuv qolardi). Shu sabab `src/lib/api.js` ga `del` qo'shildi — yagona o'zgarish, qo'shimcha.
- [2026-09-12] Serverda chat uchun "o'qildi" holati yo'q → **o'qilmaganlar qurilmadagi belgidan (`localStorage`, `src/lib/chatSeen.js`) hisoblanadi va `GET /teacher/chat/{kanal}?since=` bilan olinadi** → shu bitta so'rov ham o'qilmaganlar sonini, ham oxirgi xabar matnini beradi, so'rov esa faqat yangi xabari bor kanalga ketadi. "Bugun" va "Xabarlar" bitta funksiyadan (`channelSummaries`) foydalanadi, ya'ni ikki ekran zid raqam ko'rsatmaydi. Serverda `readAt` paydo bo'lsa — bu bitta endpointga qisqaradi.
- [2026-09-12] Hafta raqami va sanalari ikki joyda hisoblanadi (server `ScheduleMath`, ilova `src/lib/weeks.js`) → **mantiq ataylab takrorlandi** → ilova serverdan boshqa hafta ko'rsatsa jadval butunlay boshqa kunlarni chizardi; takrorlash o'rniga endpoint qo'shish backend agenti ishiga kirib borardi. Tekshirildi: 12-sentabr → 1-chorak 2-hafta (7–12 sentabr), server `meta` bilan bir xil.
- [2026-09-12] Maosh oy o'tkagichi maosh daftaridagi oylardan emas, **choraklardan** quriladi → daftar faqat hisoblangan oyni qaytaradi (demoda bittasi), o'qituvchi esa kelasi oyga ham qaraydi; u yerda "bu oyda hisob yo'q, chunki…" deb yozilgani jim to'xtab qolgan o'tkagichdan yaxshi.
- [2026-09-12] Ruxsati yo'q bo'lim (`journal`, `schedule`, `salary`, `messages`) → **pastdagi tab panelida umuman chizilmaydi** → server baribir 403 qaytaradi, ya'ni bu ko'z bo'yash emas; yopiq eshikni ko'rsatib, bosilganda "ruxsat yo'q" deyishdan foydali.
- [2026-09-12] `src/lib/session.jsx` dagi 401 ishlovchisi holatni `loading` ga qo'yadi, lekin qayta autentifikatsiya QILMAYDI — token muddati tugasa ilova "Kirilmoqda…" da qotib qoladi → **tuzatilmadi, chunki bu qobiq (ikkala panelga umumiy) fayli va parallel agent ham unga tegishi mumkin** → qobiq egasiga bitta qatorlik tuzatish sifatida topshirildi: `setUnauthorizedHandler(() => { void authenticate() })`.
- [2026-09-12] **Correction to the entry above, after the teacher panel merged:** the week math is no longer duplicated in `screens/parent/weeks.js`. Master's `src/lib/weeks.js` (teacher panel) carries the same `ScheduleMath` port, so the parent screens now import `quarterWeeks` / `weekAxis` / `addDays` / `todayISO` from there and the parent module keeps only what the shared one lacks — `parseISO` (a local-midnight `Date` for `format.js`), `lessonDow`, `weekIndexFor` (which week contains a date, falling forward across a holiday) and `calendarWeek` (Mon–Sun, for the canteen). One copy of the formula, as SPEC's own "two copies of a money formula" warning asks for.
- [2026-09-13] EduSchool is multi-branch (its `Administrator` section lists leads, students, staff and feedback across every branch at once) — do we match it? → **No. One branch, for now** (client's words: "bizda bitta filial yetadi, hozircha") → the Control Plane was deliberately removed earlier in this project and re-adding cross-branch aggregation would touch every list query, every report and the whole permission model. The `branches` table and the superadmin Filiallar screen stay, so a second branch remains a data question rather than a rewrite; what we do **not** build is the cross-branch `Administrator` module and branch-scoped filtering on every list.

## Arrears pivot — oyma-oy qarzdorlik (2026-09-16)

- [2026-09-16] "Continue the remaining work" was open-ended → **started at the top of `docs/modules/existing-module-gaps.md` §7.1** — the month-by-month arrears pivot (26 h), which that file calls "the highest value-per-hour in this entire file" → the list is already ordered by value per hour, the item needs no decision from the client, touches no financial write path and adds no table. Everything below it in §7.1 stays in that order.
- [2026-09-16] The spec sketch put the grand total inside `footer` under a `"total"` key, next to the `"YYYY-MM"` keys → **`footer` carries months only; the grand total is a separate root field `total`** → one dictionary holding both month keys and a magic string forces every consumer to filter the key set, and collides the day someone formats a month differently.
- [2026-09-16] Row order → **class name, then student name, from the server**, with a client-side "Qarz bo'yicha" toggle on the page → a pivot is read like a register, class by class; the debt-first order is one click away and needs neither a second endpoint nor a sort parameter. Rows are capped at 600, so sorting in the browser is free.
- [2026-09-16] `toBePaid` is clamped **per cell** (`max(0, amount − paid)`) and the row total is the sum of the clamped cells, not `max(0, Σamount − Σpaid)` → otherwise one overpaid month would silently cancel another month's debt and the row would stop adding up on screen. An unallocated overpayment is already visible as the `PaidWithoutAllocation` anomaly.
- [2026-09-16] Two caps, both 400 with an Uzbek message: **12 months** and **600 students** (`FinanceReportQueries.MaxArrearsMonths` / `MaxArrearsStudents`) → 600 × 12 = 7 200 cells is the edge of what a browser draws and a person reads. Beyond it the endpoint asks for a class filter instead of quietly truncating.
- [2026-09-16] Default window → **the current academic year's September through the current month** (server and page agree) → a calendar-year default opened in January would drop September–December, which is exactly where the arrears are. The page fills its own month inputs with the same rule so the request is explicit.
- [2026-09-16] A month with no invoice returns **no cell at all**, not a zero → "not enrolled that month" and "enrolled and paid in full" are different facts; the page draws the first as a grey dash and the second as a green zero.

## Channels — Telegram only (2026-09-16)

- [2026-09-16] Client, unprompted: no mobile app, no SMS, no mobile push — *"bizda hamma narsa telegram orqali bo'ladi"* → **SMS is cancelled rather than deferred, and Firebase/FCM is out of the product**; every outbound message goes through `TelegramService` / the Mini App → recorded as a hard rule in `CLAUDE.md` because it changes the scope of six separate items: the 58 h SMS block (§7.2, now **declined**), the discipline parent notification (§6.3 step 4), the news feed (§7.1 #14), `SmsModal.tsx` on the students page, `DisciplineReason.sms_template`, and the `Sozlamalar → Push (Firebase)` screen. Legacy `FcmService.cs` and that settings screen stay on disk untouched — removing them is its own task, not a side effect of this decision.

## Analitika — davomat roll-up va fanlar bo'yicha o'zlashtirish (2026-09-16)

- [2026-09-16] "Excused vs unexcused" absences with no `is_excused` column and a no-migration rule → **derived: a reason is unexcused when its name contains "sababsiz" (case-insensitive) or the school gave it a negative discipline `Points` value; everything else non-late is excused** → the seeded reason set is exactly `Kasal / Sababli / Sababsiz / Kech keldi`, so the name rule matches the data the school already types, and `AbsenceReason.Points` is an existing, client-editable field ("Intizomiy ball → Ball sabablar") that lets them steer the split without a schema change. The raw truth is not hidden behind the heuristic: the report also returns a per-reason count, so a wrong classification is visible on the same screen.
- [2026-09-16] Attendance-analytics denominator: every scheduled lesson, or only conducted ones? → **only conducted lessons (`lesson_notes.conducted = true`) × the students that lesson applies to (SubGroup)** → this is the definition `Analytics.BuildClass` and the dashboard already use. A lesson that was never held has nothing to check, so counting it would turn "the teacher was ill" into "the class was absent".
- [2026-09-16] "Late" (`AbsenceReason.IsLate`) counted inside `present`, with its own separate number → the student *was* in the lesson; folding it into absences would make the attendance rate disagree with `Analytics.BuildClass` and with the parent app. `present + absent + unchecked = opportunities` holds; `late ⊆ present`.
- [2026-09-16] By-subject attainment cell average: all (student × quarter) values weighted equally, rather than averaging per student first → **equal weight per value** → it is what the existing `grades-report/class` table shows column by column, and the two screens must not disagree about the same class. The official `QuarterGrade` still overrides the daily average, exactly as in `ClassAnalyticsController.ClassReport`.
- [2026-09-16] Both reports live next to what they measure (`Davomat` and `Baholar hisoboti`), not in an `/admin/analytics` mega-section → `docs/modules/existing-module-gaps.md` §4.2. A report you have to go and look for is a report nobody opens.

## Parity wave 1 — four screens built in parallel (2026-09-16)

- [2026-09-16] Four items from §7.1 were built at once by four agents in separate git worktrees, each forbidden to touch `App.tsx`, `navigation.ts`, `constants.ts`, `Program.cs`, migrations, `Entities.cs` or `AuditService.cs` → **the orchestrator wired every route, menu entry and permission key afterwards in one commit** → the alternative is four agents editing the same six files, which is one conflict per file and a menu that nobody owns. It cost one extra step and produced zero conflicts in those files.
- [2026-09-16] **Kassa kuni does NOT net reversals away, and that is deliberate.** `FinanceReportQueries.EffectiveAllocations` ("a reversed payment never happened") is right for debt and collection rate and wrong for a day: `LedgerService.ReverseAsync` dates the mirror rows *today*, so the money physically left the drawer today. A reversed payment therefore shows as a plus on its payment day and a minus on its reversal day, and both days stay stable forever — a daily report printed yesterday is not rewritten tonight. This is the one place where two screens show different-looking numbers for the same payment; the file header says so, and a test pins it.
- [2026-09-16] Turnstile entry/exit is **derived from the first and last pass of the day**, not read from `TurnstileEvent.Direction` → `FetchHikvisionAsync` only sets `"out"` when the device reports it, so on most installs every event lands as `"in"` and trusting the field would silently zero every exit. Same rule as `BuildStudentDashboardAsync`, so the live screen and the reports cannot disagree.
- [2026-09-16] Students with no `DeviceUserId` are **excluded from turnstile reports and counted as `unlinked`**, never as absent → the turnstile cannot see them; calling that an absence would invent a fact. It also keeps the daily report's mismatch list honest: an unlinked child is a data gap, not a discrepancy between the journal and the gate.
- [2026-09-16] Turnstile reports cap the range at **92 days** → `turnstile_events` has no index on `event_at` and every report filters on it. The honest fix is a one-line migration (`(device_user_id, event_at)`), which this wave was forbidden to write; the cap goes away when that index lands. **Follow-up, not a decision.**
- [2026-09-16] The discipline feed is a **new route (`GET /feed`), not a relaxed `GET /points`** → the feed returns a paged envelope and `/points` returns a bare array; one action cannot serve both shapes and the student profile depends on the old one. Its envelope `{ items, total, page, pageSize }` (1-based, `pageSize` clamped to 200) is the first paged list in this codebase — **reuse it verbatim** when the next one lands rather than inventing a second shape.
- [2026-09-16] Feed facets (`authors`, `classNames`) are **filter-independent** and the summary counts cover the whole filtered set rather than the page → a facet that shrinks as you filter traps the user, and header numbers that change while paging look like a bug.
- [2026-09-16] Four new test classes that each create a database per test **must clear their connection pools** (`NpgsqlConnection.ClearPool` in `DisposeAsync`) → each class was green alone and the suite failed with `53300: remaining connection slots are reserved…` once all four ran together: leaked pools ate the container's `max_connections`. `AllocationTests` already carried the warning; it is now enforced in `FinanceReportsTests`, `AttendanceAnalyticsTests`, `TurnstileAnalyticsTests`, `CashDayTests` and `StudentLedgerTests`. **A test class that creates databases owns their pools.**

## Parity wave 2 — schema first, then three slices (2026-09-16/17)

- [2026-09-16] Wave 2 needed new tables for four items at once → **one agent wrote a single migration (`ParityWave2Schema`) for all of them before any feature work began, then three feature agents built on top of it in parallel** → two migrations written concurrently both regenerate `AppDbContextModelSnapshot.cs`, and a badly resolved snapshot conflict is how an autogenerated migration ends up emitting a `DROP` against a live database. `Up()` was read line by line before merge: 5 tables, 8 columns, 14 indexes, zero drops or alters.
- [2026-09-16] The leads funnel is a **current-state snapshot**, not a flow over time → `leads` has no created-at, no source and no stage history, and nothing audits stage changes. "How many entered this month" and "days in stage" need a `lead_events` table. The page says so in an amber note instead of drawing a plausible-looking number. **Follow-up, not a decision.**
- [2026-09-17] A broken promise (`promised_on` past, debt still open) is **computed on every read and never written to `finance_anomaly_flags`** → the table's check constraints allow only the original four kinds, and a broken promise is a condition rather than an event: it clears when the parent pays, so a stored row would drift the way `students.balance` did. Consequence: it cannot be "resolved with a reason" — the button is hidden and the hint says to add an action instead.
- [2026-09-17] "Debt still open" for a promise uses the **net balance including advance payments** (`StudentBalanceQuery`) → a parent who paid ahead has kept the promise.
- [2026-09-17] Debtor actions are written under the same permission that reads them (`ViewBillingReports` — admin and director) → no new `FinanceAction` from a parallel slice, which would have meant editing the SPEC §4.3 matrix and its tests. A narrower `ManageDebtorWorkflow` is a clean follow-up if writes should be tighter than reads.
- [2026-09-17] `archive_only_non_debtor_students` (ships **on**) → archiving a pupil who owes money is refused; a **superadmin sees the refusal first and must press "Baribir arxivlash"** (`force=true`), an admin's `force` is ignored, and a bulk archive is all-or-nothing. **Known gap:** the class-level year-end archive is not gated, so it remains a way to archive a debtor. Gating it was not asked for and would block the year-end transfer.
- [2026-09-17] Archiving now **requires the free-text reason** alongside the catalogue pick → the catalogue row groups, the text carries the detail (§2.2). This is a visible behaviour change for anyone who archived with an empty reason before.
- [2026-09-17] `make_attendance_reason_required` is enforced in **`JournalService`**, so it covers the admin journal, the teacher journal and the teacher Mini App at once → there is no separate attendance status; the reason id *is* the absence.
- [2026-09-17] `is_student_grade_required` blocks ticking **"Dars o'tildi"** until every present pupil has a grade (late counts as present, absent pupils are exempt). The automatic "lesson held" mark on the first saved grade is not refused — that would block entering the first grade.
- [2026-09-17] `show_learning_progress_in_parent_dashboard` off → the grade endpoints return 403 **for parents only**, homework/journal/dashboard keep their rows but drop the grade, and the Mini App hides the Grades tab via a new `showLearningProgress` on `/api/tg/me`. Pupils and staff are unaffected.
- [2026-09-17] **The discipline parent notice goes out through Telegram only when all four hold:** the reason has `notify_parent`, the reason is active, the bot token is configured, and the parent has shared their phone with the bot. It fires once, right after a point is added by hand. Never on saving or editing a reason, never for historical points, never for attendance-derived points, never in tests (no bot token; a test asserts it). **Every reason ships with it off**, so after deploy nothing is sent until the school switches a reason on.
- [2026-09-17] **Bug found and fixed on the way: every parent call to `/api/student/*` returned 500.** `StudentPortalController.TargetAsync` normalised the phone inside the EF query, which cannot be translated — the parent grade screens in the Mini App had never worked. The match now projects `(id, parent_phone)` and compares in memory (~500 small rows per request for one school).
- [2026-09-17] **Second bug fixed:** saving a settings flag as `false` on a database with no `school_meta` row yet silently stored `true` — EF omits a `false` from the INSERT and the column default wins. The save now creates the row, then updates it; a test pins it.

## Students and Finance first (2026-09-17)

- [2026-09-17] Client: Students and Finance are "havodek muhim" and come before everything else, with every inner part of every screen → **all other work in `existing-module-gaps.md` waits**; the plan is `docs/PLAN-STUDENTS-FINANCE.md`. Menu-level parity already exists, so the first step is a screen-level audit of the 23 screens from the local bundle — columns, filters, forms, validation, actions, exports — with zero requests to the live tenant.
- [2026-09-17] "EduSchool bilan birga bir bo'lib ishlaydigan" was ambiguous between parity and a side-by-side sync → **asked; client chose feature parity** → no integration track. Had the answer been sync, the hard rule would have limited it to one-way, read-only, EduSchool → us.
- [2026-09-17] Cross-class teaching groups → **asked; client answered yes** → `Group ≠ Sinf` (~184 h) is in scope. Built in the order in PLAN §4: the membership table with `class_name` kept as a mirror and the group CRUD first (no visible change), then schedule → journal/attendance → reports/salary one at a time, cut-over last.

## Expenses and money access (2026-09-17)

- [2026-09-17] **Correction to my own claim in commit `5748139`.** I wrote that a double approval "was never a server risk" because `ApproveAsync` returns 409 `already_approved`. That was check-then-write reasoning, not a measurement. Slice FD measured it: with the guard removed, **six concurrent approvals all returned 200 and the expense posted to the ledger six times** under READ COMMITTED. The fix is a `pg_advisory_xact_lock` on the expense id with a re-read under the lock — the pattern `CashShiftService.NextReceiptNoAsync` already uses for gapless receipt numbers. **Lesson: for money, a status check inside a request is not a guard; a lock is.**
- [2026-09-17] The expense approval threshold is now read from a real endpoint (`GET /api/admin/expenses/approval-policy` → `{ threshold }`, gated on `ViewBillingReports`) instead of a constant in the client → the school can change `billing_settings.expense_approval_threshold` and the screen follows. If the call fails the page shows no number rather than a stale one. The billing-settings screen (F14.01) must edit this same setting.
- [2026-09-17] `BillingDtos.cs` carried a second, stale `CreateExpenseRequest` and `ExpenseDto` from P1-06 — no `Method`, no `Status`. Nothing referenced them (a test already had to write the namespace out in full to escape the ambiguity), so they were deleted. **Two DTOs with one name is how a client ends up written against the wrong contract**, which is exactly the shape of F1.01.
- [2026-09-17] `AdminPermAttribute` lets **any** staff account through every admin GET, and grants writes to a staff account holding the section key. That let a staff member with `teachers` **post salary money**. Ten endpoints moved to the finance role gate (salary payments, salary history/ledger, salary rates and bonuses, the salary report, and the student ledger). No new permission model, no new `FinanceAction`: salary payment is `RecordExpense`, money reads are `ViewBillingReports`. `SalaryRatesController`'s old `[AdminPerm("teachers")]` is now inert and kept only until that file's next owner removes it.
- [2026-09-17] **Still open, found while fixing the above:** `GET /api/admin/messages/telegram/registrations` returns per-student balances under `[AdminPerm("messages")]`, and `GET /api/admin/academic-year/archives/{id}/download` embeds derived balances under `[AdminPerm("academicYear")]` — any staff account reads both. Also `FinancePage` shows a staff account an empty table instead of a "closed" notice, and `PaymentHistoryModal` / `TeacherSalaryDetailModal` never catch a 403, so the spinner runs forever. None of these is fixed yet.

## Group cut-over, step 1 — schedule, salary, turnstile (2026-09-17)

- [2026-09-17] **The cut-over is one switch.** `school_meta.group_lessons_enabled` ships off; `PUT /api/admin/group-lessons` flips it, **superadmin only and audited**. Every reader (salary, turnstile, occupied slots, pupil timetable, conflicts) goes through `LessonRoster.LiveOwnersAsync`, which excludes groups while the switch is off — so a group template can be built and reviewed in advance and is invisible everywhere until the school is ready. Reversible: flip it back.
- [2026-09-17] The group id travels in the **existing `{classId}` route segment** and the existing `class_id` column → one controller, one client service and one board component instead of two parallel stacks. `LessonRoster.OwnerAsync` resolves a class first, then a group.
- [2026-09-17] **A group lesson is paid once, not once per feeding class.** Measured in `GroupSalaryTurnstileTests`: a teacher with one weekly group lesson fed by two classes earns 200 000 so'm, explicitly asserted `!= 400 000`. Before the switch that same teacher earned **0** — group lessons were silently absent from pay, which is the §2.1.5 trap.
- [2026-09-17] **A pupil cannot be double-booked.** Saving a class or group lesson that puts a pupil in two lessons in the same period is refused with 409 **and the clashing pupils are named**. Only week-assigned templates block; an unassigned template is a draft.
- [2026-09-17] `TeacherLessons.ForWeekAsync` deliberately includes **archived** owners while salary and turnstile use live ones only → today's teacher week already shows archived-class lessons, and narrowing it would have quietly removed hours from a teacher's timetable. The asymmetry is the existing behaviour, now explicit.
- [2026-09-17] `students.class_name` stays the source of truth for class rosters; `class_memberships` is written in step but not read by the resolvers. Reading it would have broken "identical to today", because the backfill only covered pupils whose class name matched a class row.
- [2026-09-17] **Known gap:** the academic-year rollover archives groups and closes memberships, but has **no test** — the rollover endpoint wipes the whole school and the harness refuses two live `ApiFactory` instances, so it needs its own database and host. Worth doing when `AcademicYearController` is next touched.
- [2026-09-17] **A dated row counts only inside the pupil's membership window**, and that rule now reaches the two controllers the `ClassAttainment` rewrite missed (`StudentProfileController` attendance range, `ClassAnalyticsController` class report). Quarter grades still call `CountsFor` without a date because they have none. Consequence, with the group switch still off: any pupil who has ever changed class now shows different attainment and attendance than yesterday — a mover's average moved 3 → 4 and attendance 100% → 67% in the agent's measurement. This is the intended behaviour, not drift, but it changes live numbers and the client should hear it before it ships.
- [2026-09-17] **Money reads need the finance right of their own.** `AdminPermAttribute` lets every staff GET through by design, so `messages/telegram/registrations` and `academic-year/archives/{id}/download` were readable by any staff member holding only their own section's permission. Both now check `finance` inside the action. The registrations list keeps working with `balance: null` (not 0 — 0 reads as "owes nothing"); the archive is refused outright, because stripping `Moliya/` would leave the balances in the pupil rows and the whole of `malumotlar.json`. This does not replace X-2; it closes the money until X-2 lands.
- [2026-09-17] **A staff member without `finance` can still send a debt reminder.** The server fills `{qarzdorlik}` when sending, so the message is correct even though the sender never sees the figure. Left as it was: the alternative is blocking debt reminders for everyone but the finance desk, which is a product decision, not a security one. Flagged for the client.
- [2026-09-17] Per-user table settings (X-1) take **no user id in the request** — not in the body, the query or the route. It always comes from the token, so there is no parameter with which to name another user's row.
- [2026-09-17] **K-6 (contract numbering mode) ships the setting but not the wiring.** `StudentContractsController.cs` was on this wave's "do not touch" list (another agent's file), so the mode lives fully in `SettingsController` (`GET|PUT /api/admin/settings/contracts`) and `ContractService` (`GetNumberModeAsync`, `Resolve` — unit-tested in `ContractNumberModeTests`), but nothing calls `Resolve` yet: `StudentContractsController.Create`/`Generate` still accept a manual number in every mode. One-line follow-up for whoever next touches that file — in `NextNumberAsync`'s two call sites, do `var decision = ContractService.Resolve(await contracts.GetNumberModeAsync(ct), req.Number); if (!decision.IsValid) return BadRequest(new { message = decision.Error }); var number = decision.ShouldGenerate ? await NextNumberAsync(ct) : decision.ManualNumber;`. Uniqueness itself already holds regardless of mode — `ux_student_contracts_number` (partial unique index) plus the existing `NumberTakenAsync` check — verified directly against the DB context in `ContractNumberModeTests.Baza_darajasidagi_unikal_indeks_raqam_takrorlanishini_rad_etadi`.
- [2026-09-17] Guruhlar (`GroupsPage.tsx`) is the first (and, for this wave, only) list wired to the new shared `DataTable` (`components/table/DataTable.tsx`, X-1 frontend). Pin is intentionally simple — pinned columns just sort to the front of the visible set, no sticky-scroll CSS — to avoid overbuilding a component the client asked to extend after the merge.
- [2026-09-17] **RESOLVED — see the entry below it for the real cause; this diagnosis was wrong in its specifics.** Open defect, found late in the night wave: `PaymentsTests.Sana_filtri_bugungi_tolovni_topadi` passes by day and fails by night. The same test fails at the pre-merge commit `0a8a2a9`, so tonight's merges did not cause it; host and Docker clocks agree, so it is not container drift. The date filter (`PaymentService.cs:461-462`, `StartOfSchoolDay` at :741) puts "today"'s upper bound at 19:00 UTC, and a payment accepted at 18:07 UTC falls outside it — which points at `Payment.ReceivedAt` carrying a Tashkent wall-clock value stored as UTC, roughly five hours ahead. If so, an evening payment files under the **next** school day and moves the cash day, the Z-report and the shift close. Handed to the agent that owns `PaymentService.cs`; `TransactionJournalQuery.cs:241` repeats the same `SchoolOffset` definition and needs the same check.
- [2026-09-17] **Storno needs the APPROVER's own open cash shift**, not the cashier's (`PaymentService.ReverseAsync` → `RequireOpenShiftAsync(approverId)`). SPEC §4.3 states only the role requirement, so a director who has never opened a shift gets an unexplained `409 no_open_shift` when reversing a payment. Found by the pupil-lifecycle walk. Not a defect — but it is a trap in a live demo, and the error message should say what to do.

## Finance parity §2.10/§2.13 — subscription-transaction reports (2026-09-17)

- [2026-09-17] §2.10 (Abonement tranzaksiyalari) was assigned as "build it", but `git merge master` pulled in commit `3503af0` (slice S1: `InvoicesController.cs`, `InvoiceService.ListPageAsync`, F10.01–F10.03) → **treated §2.10 as already closed by S1**, only F10.05 (xlsx export, P2) was missing and added → rebuilding the invoice register would have created the exact "two definitions of the same money" problem this codebase's comments warn about everywhere.
- [2026-09-17] `PermissionCheck.Finance` (`User.HasPerm`) vs `[FinanceRole(FinanceAction.ViewBillingReports)]` for the new endpoints → **used `FinanceRole`, not `PermissionCheck.Finance`** → `FinanceReportsController` and `InvoicesController` already gate on `FinanceRole` (SPEC §4.3 matrix, admin/superadmin only, cashier included in the block). `PermissionCheck.Finance` is the narrower tool for controllers whose *class-level* gate is the permissive `AdminPermAttribute` (see "Expenses and money access" above — `messages`, `academic-year`) and that incidentally expose a balance; it would have been a second, weaker gate stacked under an already-stricter one. `FinanceReportsTests.AllReports` (RBAC theory) now also covers `arrears-pivot/export`; `InvoiceRegisterTests` covers `invoices/export` the same way.
- [2026-09-17] F13.02 (toifalar bo'yicha ajratish — one row per student × category) → **implemented as an additive `SplitByCategory` flag**, not a second endpoint → `ArrearsPivotAsync` always fetches accrued/paid at category granularity now and merges in memory when the flag is off, so there is exactly one arithmetic path (the file's own top-of-file rule) and the default (unsplit) shape, JSON keys and existing tests are unchanged.
- [2026-09-17] F13.01 (EduSchool: multi-select class **and** multi-select student) → **only the class side was built** (`ClassNames`, a native `<select multiple>`); a dedicated multi-select *student* picker was not → the page already has a client-side name search over every loaded row, which answers "find these specific students" for the ≤ 600-row page the report already caps at; a proper multi-select-with-search component does not exist yet anywhere in the app, and building one for this alone is disproportionate to a P2 gap. Worth revisiting if/when a shared multi-select component is built for another screen.
- [2026-09-17] F13.04 (server paging instead of the 600-student cap) → **not built** → the 600 cap was a deliberate product decision ("Arrears pivot" entry above: "the edge of what a browser draws and a person reads"), not a backend performance limit — `FinanceReportsTests` already proves 500 students × 10 months responds in < 500 ms. Real paging would also have to make the client's search box, "Qarz bo'yicha" sort and CSV/xlsx export ("BUTUN filtr, not the page") all page-aware, which is a UX redesign, not a query change. Left for a follow-up that owns that redesign.
- [2026-09-17] F13.06 (group filter) needed `study_group_members`, listed as blocked on "PLAN §4 step B" → **unblocked**: the group-cut-over wave (`StudyGroups.cs`, see "Group cut-over, step 1" above) landed in the same merge → filter added as "current active member" (`left_on is null`), the same style `class_name` filtering already uses (today's state, not "member during the report period") — consistent with how the sinf filter itself works.
- [2026-09-17] No dedicated `api/services/studyGroups.ts` was added for the one group dropdown on the arrears page → fetched inline in `financeReports`'s page component against `GET /admin/study-groups` → avoids guessing the shape of a shared groups client another, unrelated wave may still be building; the dropdown only needs `{id, name, isArchived}`.


## Money hardening — subscription end preview, payment auto-receipt, allocation lock (2026-09-17, F1.06/F1.07/F1.10)

- [2026-09-17] **F1.06 end-with-preview is a NEW read endpoint plus the EXISTING void/end endpoints, not one atomic transaction.** `finance-parity.md` describes voiding the selected future invoices "in the same transaction" as ending the subscription. `BillingCatalogController.cs` (owns `POST …/subscriptions/{id}/end`) and `InvoiceService.cs`'s `VoidAsync` are both another agent's territory tonight; wrapping them into one transaction would have meant either editing the off-limits controller or changing `VoidAsync`'s transaction ownership more than a narrow lock-key exposure. Chose three separate calls instead: `POST …/subscriptions/{id}/end/preview` (new, read-only) → existing `POST …/invoices/{id}/void` once per selected month → existing `POST …/subscriptions/{id}/end`. Each call is independently atomic and correctly locked; a crash between steps leaves a partial-but-safe state (some months voided, subscription not yet ended, or vice versa) that the admin can simply finish by retrying — voiding an already-void invoice fails loud (`already_void`), it never double-posts. The settlement rule itself (end month never prorated, only strictly-later months ever appear in preview) is unaffected by this choice.
- [2026-09-17] **F1.06 preview reuses `InvoiceDto`/`canVoid()` instead of inventing a `Voidable` flag.** `IInvoiceService.ListAsync` already derives `Paid`/`Remaining`/`Status` server-side, and `api/services/invoices.ts` already has `canVoid(invoice) = status !== 'void' && paid <= 0`.
